<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Mézard–Parisi hierarchical cavity formula for diluted even-arity Ising models in the Panchenko–Talagrand class. This resolves the variational equality conjecture for that class: the limiting pressure equals the infimum of the trial functional over all finite hierarchy depths and trial laws. Only first moments of the interaction and external field are required.

- **claim-main-formula** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): Under <a href="#model:integrability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:integrability">[model:integrability]</a>–<a href="#model:positivity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:positivity">[model:positivity]</a>, the thermodynamic limit exists and $$\label{main:formula} \lim_{N\to\infty}F_N=\inf_{r\ge0}\Phi_r.$$
  - Explanation: The limiting pressure exists and equals the infimum, over all finite hierarchy depths and trial laws, of the hierarchical cavity functional. The proof combines an interpolation upper bound with a cavity lower bound.
- **claim-insertion-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-insertion)): Suppose a leaf partition function is iterated by power means, with exponents $m_1,\ldots,m_{L-1}$, and $K_1,\ldots,K_{L-1}$ are its tilted kernels. Let $K_L$ be its terminal Gibbs kernel. Multiplying each spin weight by $A$ changes the iterated root logarithm by $\log Y_0$, where <a href="#tree:Y-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:Y-recursion">[tree:Y-recursion]</a> uses the kernels before insertion. The new kernels are <a href="#tree:updated-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:updated-kernel">[tree:updated-kernel]</a>.* *For an internal vertex $\beta$ of a prescribed tree, let $d_\beta$ be its depth, let $n_\beta$ be its number of children, and put $$\gamma_\beta=m_{d_\beta}-n_\beta m_{d_\beta+1}.$$ The conditional joint density of its updated tree law is $$\label{tree:density} W_{\mathcal T}(A) :=\frac{dP_{\mathcal T}^A}{dP_{\mathcal T}} =\prod_{a\in\operatorname{Leaves}(\mathcal T)}A^a \prod_{\beta\in\operatorname{Int}(\mathcal T)} Y_\beta^{\gamma_\beta}.$$ Here $Y_\beta$ is $Y_{d_\beta}$ evaluated on the prefix at $\beta$.* *The recursion is monotone and homogeneous: if $a\leq A\leq b$, for positive root-measurable $a,b$, then $a\leq Y_d\leq b$ at every depth; and multiplying $A$ by a positive root-measurable constant multiplies every $Y_d$ by that constant.
  - Explanation: Multiplying each spin weight by a positive factor shifts the iterated root logarithm by a recursively defined quantity, and the tilted kernels update by explicit density ratios. The updated tree density is a product of leaf factors and internal-vertex factors.
- **claim-log-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-log-expansion)): For a bounded real path function $D$ and $A=1+tD$, $$\label{tree:log-series} \log Y_0 =\sum_{k\geq1}\frac{(-1)^{k-1}t^k}{k} \mathbb E_{Q_k}E_{\mathcal T} \prod_{a=1}^k D^a, \qquad \|t\|\\|D\\|_\infty<1.$$ This identity is conditional on the root. If $\|t\|\\|D\\|_\infty\leq c<1$, the absolute value of the remainder after order $K$ is at most $$\label{tree:log-tail} \sum_{k>K}\frac{c^k}{k}.$$
  - Explanation: The insertion logarithm is an alternating power series whose coefficients are expectations over randomly grown replica trees, with an explicit geometric tail bound.
- **claim-canceled-anchor-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-extension-expansion)): Let $H$ be a bounded function of the variables on the old tree, fixed as the insertion parameters vary. For $A_{\boldsymbol t}=1+\sum_{j=1}^k t_jD_j$, with bounded real path functions $D_j$, one has $$\label{tree:mixed-expansion} \left.\partial_{t_1}\cdots\partial_{t_k} E_{\mathcal T}^{A_{\boldsymbol t}} \frac{H}{A_{\boldsymbol t}^{a_*}} \right\|_{\boldsymbol t=0} =\sum_E c_E\, E_{\mathcal T_E} \left[H\prod_{j=1}^kD_j^{a_j(E)}\right].$$ The formula holds in any fixed order of the colors. For identical directions $D_j=D$, the coefficient of $t^k$ is the right-hand side divided by $k!$. The analytic bounds established above apply to its left-hand side, uniformly over the path kernels. The empty extension has weight $1$.
  - Explanation: Mixed derivatives of an expectation with a canceled anchor denominator are sums over extension histories, each weighted by the product of its coefficients.
- **claim-reference-path-totals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-reference-total)): Suppose the next color must follow a specified reference prefix to depth $d$ and then enter a child distinct from all $j\geq1$ children there that contain reference paths. Apart from these ancestry constraints its continuation is unrestricted. The sum of the coefficients of all allowed choices is $$\label{tree:one-reference-total} m_d-jm_{d+1}.$$ Consequently, if the anchor and the colors must have a prescribed tree $S$, the total coefficient over all compatible extensions of any old tree is $$\label{tree:K} \kappa(S)=\prod_{v\in\operatorname{Br}(S)} \prod_{j=1}^{a_v-1}(m_{d_v}-jm_{d_v+1}),$$ where $a_v$ and $d_v$ are the child count and depth of each branching vertex in the prescribed reference tree. More generally, the same one-step rule can be iterated starting from any already constructed set of references.
  - Explanation: The signed sum of extension coefficients under ancestry constraints is an explicit product over branching vertices of the prescribed reference tree.
- **claim-charging-fresh-splits** (bound, [source](#stmt-tree-charging)): Assume $m_d=d/L$ and put $\delta=L^{-1}$. Start with an old tree having $q$ leaves and assign $k$ colors by the canceled-anchor extension rule. Prescribe the reference shape of the anchor and the colors, possibly with additional restrictions on allowed extensions. Suppose $b$ specified branching vertices of that shape have depths which are not branching depths anywhere in the old tree. Then $$\label{tree:charging-bound} \sum_{E\text{ allowed}}\|c_E\|\leq C_{q,k}\delta^b.$$ The constant is independent of $L$, the prescribed depths, and their coincidences at different vertices.* *In particular, for these $b$ vertices let $$J=\prod_v\prod_{j=1}^{a_v-1}(m_{d_v}-jm_{d_v+1}).$$ If $m_{d_v}\geq\eta>0$ at all of them, then $J\ne0$ and $$\label{tree:relative-charging} \frac{1}{\|J\|}\sum_{E\text{ allowed}}\|c_E\| \leq C_{q,k,\eta}.$$
  - Explanation: Each target branching vertex that must be created by a fresh unary split contributes one small factor to the sum of absolute coefficients, which also gives a relative bound after division by the reference coefficient.
- **claim-regular-shapes** (bound, [source](#stmt-tree-regular-shapes)): For every fixed $k$ there is $C_k<\infty$, independent of $L$ and $\eta$, such that $$\label{tree:irregular-probability} Q_k\{\mathcal T\text{ is not regular}\} \leq C_k(\eta+L^{-1}).$$ For any particular labeled topology with $a$ branching vertices and any consistent assignment of their depths, $$\label{tree:assignment-probability} Q_k\{\text{that topology and depth assignment}\} \leq C_kL^{-a}.$$ Deleting leaves from a regular tree produces a regular tree, after discarding branching vertices that become unary.
  - Explanation: Non-regular trees have small probability under the tree law, each prescribed topology with depth assignment has probability controlled by a power of the inverse depth, and regularity survives leaf deletion.
- **claim-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-bound)): Under the hypotheses of <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>, $F_N\le\mathcal B_r(\zeta,\mathbf m)$ for every $N$ and every admissible finite-level trial law. Consequently $F_N\le\inf_{r\ge0}\Phi_r$.
  - Explanation: Interpolation and convexity of an even power show that the pressure at every system size is below the trial functional for every admissible trial law, hence below the infimum.
- **claim-cavity-increment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cavity-increment)): The increments $\Delta_N^{L,\mathbf u}=P_{N+1}^{L,\mathbf u}-P_N^{L,\mathbf u}$ are uniformly bounded in $N,L,\mathbf u$. There is a reservoir on $N$ spins with physical interaction count of mean $$\label{cavity:reservoir-mean} \rho_N=\alpha(N+1)\left(\frac{N}{N+1}\right)^p,$$ the usual fields, and the perturbation counts of means $c_\nu s_N$, such that $$\label{cavity:identity} \Delta_N^{L,\mathbf u} =\log2+\mathbb E\log Y_{0,\rm s}-\mathbb E\log Y_{0,\rm b}+o_N(1).$$ The error is uniform in $L,\mathbf u$. The insertion recursion uses the tilted reservoir kernels and independent new root disorder, with terminal insertions $$\begin{aligned} A_{\rm s}(\sigma)&=\frac12\sum_{\varepsilon=\pm1} \exp\left(h\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\rm s}} \theta_j(\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\ldots,\sigma_{i_{p-1,j}},\varepsilon)\right), \label{cavity:site-insertion}\\ A_{\rm b}(\sigma)&=\exp\left(\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\rm b}} \theta_j(\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\ldots,\sigma_{i_{p,j}})\right), \label{cavity:bond-insertion} \end{aligned}$$ where $K_{\rm s}\sim\mathop{\mathrm{Poisson}}(\alpha p)$, $K_{\rm b}\sim\mathop{\mathrm{Poisson}}(\alpha(p-1))$, and all displayed indices are independent uniform indices in $[N]$.
  - Explanation: Pressure increments of the perturbed system are uniformly bounded and equal, up to a vanishing error, to a site insertion minus a bond insertion on a common reservoir.
- **claim-score-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pert-concentration)): For each fixed $L$ and each fixed type $\nu$, $$\int e_{\nu,N}(\mathbf u)\,\pi(d\mathbf u)\longrightarrow0. \label{pert:concentration-eq}$$ The assertion holds for the reservoir as well as for the original system. Constants need not be uniform in $L$ or $\nu$.
  - Explanation: Averaged over the perturbation parameters, the thermal and root fluctuations of each perturbation score vanish, for the reservoir as well as the original system.
- **claim-parameter-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pert-selection)): For every fixed $L\geq2$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there are sizes $N_j\uparrow\infty$ and deterministic vectors $\mathbf u_j$ such that* 1. *$\Delta_{N_j}(\mathbf u_j)\leq \underline F+2\varepsilon$;* 2. *for every fixed type $\nu$, $e_{\nu,N_j}(\mathbf u_j)\longrightarrow0$ in the reservoir;* 3. *in the reservoir, the expectations of every finite product of multioverlaps on every fixed finite prescribed tree of depth $L$ converge along this sequence.
  - Explanation: A sequence of sizes and deterministic parameters exists along which increments are near the liminf, all score errors vanish, and all finite-tree multioverlap moments converge.
- **claim-palm-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pert-palm)): If $\nu$ is a type with the specified marks, then $$\|H_N(t_\nu,u_\nu)\|\leq\\|f\\|_\infty e_{\nu,N}. \label{pert:palm-bound}$$ For a fixed mark specification the function $H_N(t,u)$ is the same for all its types $j$: its base reservoir contains the full original Poisson families, including all types.
  - Explanation: The Poisson add-one identity turns the score covariance into an analytic difference function evaluated at the type parameters, with error bounded by the score error.
- **claim-vanishing-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pert-analytic)): For every mark specification included in the perturbation family, old tree, anchor, and bounded overlap-polynomial test $f$, every Taylor coefficient at $t=0$ of $H_N(t,0)$ tends to zero along the selected sequence.
  - Explanation: Every Taylor coefficient at the origin of the analytic difference function tends to zero along the selected sequence, by comparing with very small parameter values.
- **claim-tree-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pert-identity)): For the perturbation family defined above, along every sequence from Lemma <a href="#pert:selection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pert:selection">12</a>, $$\sum_{E\in\mathfrak E(\mathcal T,a_*,\mathcal C)} c_E\,\mathbb E[fR_E] -\mathbb E f \sum_{E\in\mathfrak E(\{a_*\},a_*,\mathcal C)} c_E\,\mathbb E[R_E] \longrightarrow0. \label{pert:tree-identity-eq}$$ This holds for every fixed old tree, either kind of anchor, every bounded polynomial $f$ in its multioverlaps, every finite number of ordered added colors, and every prescribed constraint $\mathcal C$. The empty addition is allowed. The error is taken to zero at fixed $L$, fixed tree, and fixed constraint, before any limit in $L$.
  - Explanation: Signed sums of multioverlap moments over extension histories satisfy an asymptotic factorization identity for every prescribed colored tree constraint, at fixed depth.
- **claim-overlap-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-decor-overlap-concentration)): For every fixed labeled topology $S$, $$\lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty} \mathcal A_{L,\eta}\operatorname{Var}(R_S)=0 \qquad(\eta>0), \label{decor:overlap-concentration-equation}$$ where only the $b(S)$ branching depths are averaged. For a singleton, whose average has no coordinates, $\operatorname{Var}(R_S)\to0$ as $N\to\infty$ at every fixed $L$.
  - Explanation: The variance of every multioverlap, averaged over separated interior branching depths, vanishes in the ordered limits; for a singleton it vanishes at each fixed depth.
- **claim-contractions** (bound, [source](#stmt-decor-contractions)): If a random vector $B\in[-1,1]^N$ is conditionally independent of $A_S$ given $\mathscr F_d$, then $$\bigl\\|(A_S-\mu_{d,S})\cdot B\bigr\\|_{L^2} \le v_N(S,d)^{1/4}. \label{decor:contraction-bound}$$ If $X,Y,Z$ are conditionally independent copies of a bounded random vector given a sigma field $\mathscr G$, and $C$ is its conditional covariance matrix, then $$\mathbb E(X\cdot Y-X\cdot Z)^2 \ge \frac2{N^2}\mathbb E\sum_{i,j}C_{ij}^2. \label{decor:three-copy-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Contractions against conditionally independent bounded vectors are controlled by the squared conditional covariance, and a three-copy estimate bounds that covariance from below.
- **claim-depth-continuity** (bound, [source](#stmt-decor-depth-continuity)): Fix a topology $S$ and a subset $U$ of its branching vertices. For each $\eta$-regular depth assignment, let $S^U$ have the same topology with the depths of the vertices in $U$ increased by one. At an ancestral evaluation depth $d$, compare the means using the same sampled prefix. Then, for all sufficiently large $L$, $$\mathcal A_{L,\eta}\mathbb E \\|\mu_{d,S^U}-\mu_{d,S}\\|_N^2\le C/L. \label{decor:branch-depth-continuity}$$ The average ranges over all branching depths; the coordinate $d$ is also averaged if it ranges, and otherwise is held fixed. If $d$ ranges along an initial unary stretch of $S$, then $$\mathcal A_{L,\eta}\mathbb E \\|\mu_{d+1,S}-\mu_{d,S}\\|_N^2\le C/L, \label{decor:evaluation-depth-continuity}$$ where this average includes $d$ and all branching depths, and the means are evaluated along the same sampled path. The constant depends only on the number of vertices and shifts, uniformly in $N$, the evaluation depth, and the transition kernels.
  - Explanation: Delaying branching depths by one level, or moving the evaluation depth by one level, changes conditional means by a small amount on depth average.
- **claim-conditional-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-decor-conditional-concentration)): For every fixed labeled topology $S$ and every $\eta>0$, $$\lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty} \mathcal A_{L,\eta}v_N(S,d)=0, \label{decor:conditional-concentration-equation}$$ where the average ranges over all branching depths of $S$ and an additional regular depth $d$ strictly before its first split. For a singleton, the average ranges over $d$ alone.
  - Explanation: The squared conditional covariance of spin products below a sampled prefix vanishes on depth average, proved by induction on the number of leaves.
- **claim-law-encoding** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-encoding)): For each fixed $N$, $L\ge2$, and selected parameter vector, with the reservoir root disorder averaged, there is a deterministic trial law $\zeta_{L,N}\in\mathcal M_L$ whose single-label spin process has the same expected spin products on every prescribed tree as a uniformly chosen site of the reservoir. Different trial labels can be chosen independently.
  - Explanation: A deterministic nested trial law reproduces all expected spin products of a uniformly chosen reservoir site on every prescribed tree, and independent copies give independent labels.
- **claim-pattern-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-patterns)): Fix a prescribed tree with $k$ leaves. Suppose an observable of absolute value at most one uses the replica-spin patterns at $a$ independent uniform site labels. Replacing these patterns by $a$ independent trial-label patterns from <a href="#comp:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:encoding">22</a> changes its expected value by at most $$\label{comp:pattern-error} a\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq[k]}\mathbb E\|R_S-\mathbb ER_S\|.$$ The observable may also depend on new insertion disorder, independent of the reservoir.
  - Explanation: Replacing replica-spin patterns at uniform sites by independent trial-label patterns changes a bounded observable by at most a sum of multioverlap deviations.
- **claim-insertion-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-insertion-comparison)): For the trial laws of <a href="#comp:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:encoding">22</a>, with $\mathbf m^{(L)}=(1/L,\ldots,(L-1)/L)$, $$\label{comp:increment-comparison} \lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty} \left\|\Delta_N^{L,\mathbf u_{L,N}} -\mathcal B_{L-1}(\zeta_{L,N},\mathbf m^{(L)})\right\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: In the ordered limits the reservoir cavity increment and the trial functional of the encoded law agree.
- **claim-bounded-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-bounded-lower)): For every bounded permutation-invariant interaction law and bounded field law, with the functionals defined in <a href="#model:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:section">2</a>, $$\liminf_{N\to\infty}F_N\ge\inf_{r\ge0}\Phi_r.$$
  - Explanation: For bounded permutation-invariant interactions and bounded fields, the liminf of the pressure is at least the infimum of the trial functionals.
- **claim-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-reduction-stability)): Couple two interaction laws $\theta,\theta'$ and two field laws $h,h'$, each satisfying <a href="#model:integrability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:integrability">[model:integrability]</a> for the fixed $p$ and $\alpha$, and put $D=\mathbb E\max_s\|\theta(s)-\theta'(s)\|$ and $d=\mathbb E\|h-h'\|$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \|F_N-F_N'\|&\le\alpha D+d,\label{reduction:free-stability}\\ \|\mathcal B_r(\zeta,\mathbf m)-\mathcal B_r'(\zeta,\mathbf m)\| &\le\alpha(2p-1)D+d\label{reduction:trial-stability} \end{aligned}$$ uniformly in $N,r$ and all trial laws, including $r=0$. If $c$ is an integrable random constant associated with an interaction and $\widehat\theta=\theta-c$, then $$\label{reduction:shift} \widehat F_N=F_N-\alpha\mathbb Ec, \qquad\widehat{\mathcal B}_r=\mathcal B_r-\alpha\mathbb Ec.$$
  - Explanation: The pressure and the trial functional are Lipschitz in the interaction and field laws, uniformly in size, depth and trial law, and an integrable constant shift moves both by the same amount.
- **claim-viana-bray-even-spin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-models-spin)): Fix an even integer $p\ge2$, $\alpha>0$, and independent real random variables $J,H$ such that $J$ has a symmetric distribution and $\mathbb E\|J\|+\mathbb E\|H\|<\infty$. For every $\beta>0$, the model with $$\theta_\beta(s_1,\ldots,s_p)=\beta J\prod_{\ell=1}^p s_\ell, \qquad h_\beta=\beta H$$ satisfies the variational formula <a href="#main:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:formula">[main:formula]</a>, with the trial functional evaluated at these interaction and field laws. In particular, this holds for the Viana–Bray model (Viana and Bray 1985) [[VB85](#ref-VB85)], which is the case $p=2$.
  - Explanation: The variational formula holds for diluted even-spin models with symmetric couplings, including the Viana–Bray model, at every positive temperature.
- **claim-soft-even-sat** (theorem, [source](#stmt-models-sat)): Fix an even integer $K\ge2$ and $\alpha>0$. Let $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_K$ be independent fair signs and let $W\ge0$ be independent of the signs, with $\mathbb EW<\infty$. For every $\beta>0$, the interaction $$\theta_\beta(s_1,\ldots,s_K) =-\beta W\prod_{\ell=1}^K\frac{1+\varepsilon_\ell s_\ell}{2}, \qquad h=0,$$ satisfies <a href="#main:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:formula">[main:formula]</a>. The choice $W=1$ is the soft random even-$K$ SAT model; $W$ gives an independent nonnegative clause weight.
  - Explanation: The variational formula holds for soft random even-arity satisfiability with independent nonnegative clause weights at every positive temperature.
- **claim-ground-state-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-models-ground)): For each model in [models:spin,models:sat], the limit $$g=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\mathbb E\max_\sigma\mathcal E_N(\sigma)$$ exists and satisfies $$\label{models:ground-formula} g=\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac1\beta \inf_{r\ge0}\inf_{\zeta,\mathbf m} \mathcal B_{r,\beta}(\zeta,\mathbf m).$$ More precisely, the expression inside the temperature limit lies in $[g,g+\log(2)/\beta]$. For unit-weight SAT, the limiting expected minimum number of violated clauses per variable is $-g$.
  - Explanation: The ground-state energy density exists and equals the zero-temperature limit of the scaled variational infimum, with an explicit sandwich at each temperature.

For $A_{\\boldsymbol t}=1+\\sum_{j=1}^k t_jD_j$, with bounded real path functions $D_j$, one has $$\\label{tree:mixed-expansion}\n  \\left.\\partial_{t_1}\\cdots\\partial_{t_k}\n       E_{\\mathcal T}^{A_{\\boldsymbol t}}\n        \\frac{H}{A_{\\boldsymbol t}^{a_*}}\n  \\right|_{\\boldsymbol t=0}\n   =\\sum_E c_E\\,\n       E_{\\mathcal T_E}\n          \\left[H\\prod_{j=1}^kD_j^{a_j(E)}\\right].$$ The formula holds in any fixed order of the colors. For identical directions $D_j=D$, the coefficient of $t^k$ is the right-hand side divided by $k!$. The analytic bounds established above apply to its left-hand side, uniformly over the path kernels. The empty extension has weight $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-221.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $H$ be a bounded function of the variables on the old tree, fixed as the insertion parameters vary. For identical directions $D_j=D$, the coefficient of $t^k$ is the right-hand side divided by $k!$. The analytic bounds established above apply to its left-hand side, uniformly over the path kernels.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tree-reference-total",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the next color must follow a specified reference prefix to depth $d$ and then enter a child distinct from all $j\\geq1$ children there that contain reference paths. Apart from these ancestry constraints its continuation is unrestricted. The sum of the coefficients of all allowed choices is $$\\label{tree:one-reference-total}\n  m_d-jm_{d+1}.$$ Consequently, if the anchor and the colors must have a prescribed tree $S$, the total coefficient over all compatible extensions of any old tree is $$\\label{tree:K}\n \\kappa(S)=\\prod_{v\\in\\operatorname{Br}(S)}\n           \\prod_{j=1}^{a_v-1}(m_{d_v}-jm_{d_v+1}),$$ where $a_v$ and $d_v$ are the child count and depth of each branching vertex in the prescribed reference tree. More generally, the same one-step rule can be iterated starting from any already constructed set of references.",
        "source_id": "PM-221.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-tree-reference-total",

          "source_id": "PM-221.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tree-charging",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $m_d=d/L$ and put $\\delta=L^{-1}$. Start with an old tree having $q$ leaves and assign $k$ colors by the canceled-anchor extension rule. Prescribe the reference shape of the anchor and the colors, possibly with additional restrictions on allowed extensions. Suppose $b$ specified branching vertices of that shape have depths which are not branching depths anywhere in the old tree. Then $$\\label{tree:charging-bound}\n   \\sum_{E\\text{ allowed}}|c_E|\\leq C_{q,k}\\delta^b.$$ The constant is independent of $L$, the prescribed depths, and their coincidences at different vertices.*\n\n*In particular, for these $b$ vertices let $$J=\\prod_v\\prod_{j=1}^{a_v-1}(m_{d_v}-jm_{d_v+1}).$$ If $m_{d_v}\\geq\\eta>0$ at all of them, then $J\\ne0$ and $$\\label{tree:relative-charging}\n   \\frac{1}{|J|}\\sum_{E\\text{ allowed}}|c_E|\n        \\leq C_{q,k,\\eta}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-221.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

Suppose $b$ specified branching vertices of that shape have depths which are not branching depths anywhere in the old tree. Then $$\\label{tree:charging-bound}\n   \\sum_{E\\text{ allowed}}|c_E|\\leq C_{q,k}\\delta^b.$$ The constant is independent of $L$, the prescribed depths, and their coincidences at different vertices.*\n\n*In particular, for these $b$ vertices let $$J=\\prod_v\\prod_{j=1}^{a_v-1}(m_{d_v}-jm_{d_v+1}).$$ If $m_{d_v}\\geq\\eta>0$ at all of them, then $J\\ne0$ and $$\\label{tree:relative-charging}\n   \\frac{1}{|J|}\\sum_{E\\text{ allowed}}|c_E|\n        \\leq C_{q,k,\\eta}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tree-regular-shapes",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $k$ there is $C_k<\\infty$, independent of $L$ and $\\eta$, such that $$\\label{tree:irregular-probability}\n  Q_k\\{\\mathcal T\\text{ is not regular}\\}\n        \\leq C_k(\\eta+L^{-1}).$$ For any particular labeled topology with $a$ branching vertices and any consistent assignment of their depths, $$\\label{tree:assignment-probability}\n  Q_k\\{\\text{that topology and depth assignment}\\}\n       \\leq C_kL^{-a}.$$ Deleting leaves from a regular tree produces a regular tree, after discarding branching vertices that become unary.",
        "source_id": "PM-221.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-221.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $k$ there is $C_k<\\infty$, independent of $L$ and $\\eta$, such that $$\\label{tree:irregular-probability}\n  Q_k\\{\\mathcal T\\text{ is not regular}\\}\n        \\leq C_k(\\eta+L^{-1}).$$ For any particular labeled topology with $a$ branching vertices and any consistent assignment of their depths, $$\\label{tree:assignment-probability}\n  Q_k\\{\\text{that topology and depth assignment}\\}\n       \\leq C_kL^{-a}.$$ Deleting leaves from a regular tree produces a regular tree, after discarding branching vertices that become unary.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cavity-increment",
        "exact_quote": "The increments $\\Delta_N^{L,\\mathbf u}=P_{N+1}^{L,\\mathbf u}-P_N^{L,\\mathbf u}$ are uniformly bounded in $N,L,\\mathbf u$. There is a reservoir on $N$ spins with physical interaction count of mean $$\\label{cavity:reservoir-mean}\n \\rho_N=\\alpha(N+1)\\left(\\frac{N}{N+1}\\right)^p,$$ the usual fields, and the perturbation counts of means $c_\\nu s_N$, such that $$\\label{cavity:identity}\n \\Delta_N^{L,\\mathbf u}\n =\\log2+\\mathbb E\\log Y_{0,\\rm s}-\\mathbb E\\log Y_{0,\\rm b}+o_N(1).$$ The error is uniform in $L,\\mathbf u$. The insertion recursion uses the tilted reservoir kernels and independent new root disorder, with terminal insertions $$\\begin{aligned}\n A_{\\rm s}(\\sigma)&=\\frac12\\sum_{\\varepsilon=\\pm1}\n \\exp\\left(h\\varepsilon+\\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\\rm s}}\n \\theta_j(\\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\\ldots,\\sigma_{i_{p-1,j}},\\varepsilon)\\right),\n \\label{cavity:site-insertion}\\\\\n A_{\\rm b}(\\sigma)&=\\exp\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\\rm b}}\n \\theta_j(\\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\\ldots,\\sigma_{i_{p,j}})\\right),\n \\label{cavity:bond-insertion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $K_{\\rm s}\\sim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Poisson}}(\\alpha p)$, $K_{\\rm b}\\sim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Poisson}}(\\alpha(p-1))$, and all displayed indices are independent uniform indices in $[N]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-221.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cavity-increment",
          "exact_quote": "The increments $\\Delta_N^{L,\\mathbf u}=P_{N+1}^{L,\\mathbf u}-P_N^{L,\\mathbf u}$ are uniformly bounded in $N,L,\\mathbf u$. There is a reservoir on $N$ spins with physical interaction count of mean $$\\label{cavity:reservoir-mean}\n \\rho_N=\\alpha(N+1)\\left(\\frac{N}{N+1}\\right)^p,$$ the usual fields, and the perturbation counts of means $c_\\nu s_N$, such that $$\\label{cavity:identity}\n \\Delta_N^{L,\\mathbf u}\n =\\log2+\\mathbb E\\log Y_{0,\\rm s}-\\mathbb E\\log Y_{0,\\rm b}+o_N(1).$$ The error is uniform in $L,\\mathbf u$. The insertion recursion uses the tilted reservoir kernels and independent new root disorder, with terminal insertions $$\\begin{aligned}\n A_{\\rm s}(\\sigma)&=\\frac12\\sum_{\\varepsilon=\\pm1}\n \\exp\\left(h\\varepsilon+\\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\\rm s}}\n \\theta_j(\\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\\ldots,\\sigma_{i_{p-1,j}},\\varepsilon)\\right),\n \\label{cavity:site-insertion}\\\\\n A_{\\rm b}(\\sigma)&=\\exp\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\\rm b}}\n \\theta_j(\\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\\ldots,\\sigma_{i_{p,j}})\\right),\n \\label{cavity:bond-insertion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $K_{\\rm s}\\sim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Poisson}}(\\alpha p)$, $K_{\\rm b}\\sim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Poisson}}(\\alpha(p-1))$, and all displayed indices are independent uniform indices in $[N]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-221.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The increments $\\Delta_N^{L,\\mathbf u}=P_{N+1}^{L,\\mathbf u}-P_N^{L,\\mathbf u}$ are uniformly bounded in $N,L,\\mathbf u$. There is a reservoir on $N$ spins with physical interaction count of mean $$\\label{cavity:reservoir-mean}\n \\rho_N=\\alpha(N+1)\\left(\\frac{N}{N+1}\\right)^p,$$ the usual fields, and the perturbation counts of means $c_\\nu s_N$, such that $$\\label{cavity:identity}\n \\Delta_N^{L,\\mathbf u}\n =\\log2+\\mathbb E\\log Y_{0,\\rm s}-\\mathbb E\\log Y_{0,\\rm b}+o_N(1).$$ The error is uniform in $L,\\mathbf u$. The insertion recursion uses the tilted reservoir kernels and independent new root disorder, with terminal insertions $$\\begin{aligned}\n A_{\\rm s}(\\sigma)&=\\frac12\\sum_{\\varepsilon=\\pm1}\n \\exp\\left(h\\varepsilon+\\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\\rm s}}\n \\theta_j(\\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\\ldots,\\sigma_{i_{p-1,j}},\\varepsilon)\\right),\n \\label{cavity:site-insertion}\\\\\n A_{\\rm b}(\\sigma)&=\\exp\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{K_{\\rm b}}\n \\theta_j(\\sigma_{i_{1,j}},\\ldots,\\sigma_{i_{p,j}})\\right),\n \\label{cavity:bond-insertion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $K_{\\rm s}\\sim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Poisson}}(\\alpha p)$, $K_{\\rm b}\\sim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Poisson}}(\\alpha(p-1))$, and all displayed indices are independent uniform indices in $[N]$.",
      "explanation": "Pressure increments of the perturbed system are uniformly bounded and equal, up to a vanishing error, to a site insertion minus a bond insertion on a common reservoir.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pert-selection",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $L\\geq2$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, there are sizes $N_j\\uparrow\\infty$ and deterministic vectors $\\mathbf u_j$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\Delta_{N_j}(\\mathbf u_j)\\leq \\underline F+2\\varepsilon$;*\n\n2.  *for every fixed type $\\nu$, $e_{\\nu,N_j}(\\mathbf u_j)\\longrightarrow0$ in the reservoir;*\n\n3.  *in the reservoir, the expectations of every finite product of multioverlaps on every fixed finite prescribed tree of depth $L$ converge along this sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-221.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-pert-selection",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $L\\geq2$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, there are sizes $N_j\\uparrow\\infty$ and deterministic vectors $\\mathbf u_j$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\Delta_{N_j}(\\mathbf u_j)\\leq \\underline F+2\\varepsilon$;*\n\n2.  *for every fixed type $\\nu$, $e_{\\nu,N_j}(\\mathbf u_j)\\longrightarrow0$ in the reservoir;*\n\n3.  *in the reservoir, the expectations of every finite product of multioverlaps on every fixed finite prescribed tree of depth $L$ converge along this sequence.",
          "source_id": "PM-221.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $L\\geq2$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, there are sizes $N_j\\uparrow\\infty$ and deterministic vectors $\\mathbf u_j$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\Delta_{N_j}(\\mathbf u_j)\\leq \\underline F+2\\varepsilon$;*\n\n2.  *for every fixed type $\\nu$, $e_{\\nu,N_j}(\\mathbf u_j)\\longrightarrow0$ in the reservoir;*\n\n3.  *in the reservoir, the expectations of every finite product of multioverlaps on every fixed finite prescribed tree of depth $L$ converge along this sequence.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pert-palm",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **pressure** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We call this quantity *pressure*.
- **multioverlap** ([source](#pm-h-2)): The spatial average of such a product is a *multioverlap*; pair overlaps alone do not determine all these patterns.
- **message distribution q_x** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For $x\in\mathbb R$ let $q_x(s)=e^{xs}/(2\cosh x)$.
- **nested measure spaces** ([source](#model:chain)): Let $\mathcal M_1=\mathcal P(\mathbb R)$ and $\mathcal M_{j+1}=\mathcal P(\mathcal M_j)$, equipped with the topology of weak convergence and its Borel sigma-field.
- **protected leaf** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Call a leaf *protected* if its factor $A^a$ in the tree density is canceled by a denominator $A^a$.
- **regular shape** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For $0<\eta<1/2$, call a shape *regular* if every branching depth divided by $L$ lies in $(\eta,1-\eta)$ and any two distinct branching vertices have normalized depths differing by more than $\eta$.
- **reservoir** ([source](#cavity:section)): We compare systems of sizes $N$ and $N+1$ through a common $N$-spin system, the *reservoir*.
- **mark specification** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A *mark specification* consists of a finite number $n\geq0$ of labeled Rademacher coordinates, a depth in $\{1,\ldots,L-1\}$ assigned to each coordinate, two functions $g,g_0:\{-1,1\}^n\to\mathbb Q\cap[-1,1]$, and a choice $S_0=1$ or $S_0=\sigma_i$.
- **spin anchor and marker anchor** ([source](#pm-h-26)): The anchor is called a *spin anchor* when $S_0=\sigma_i$, and a *marker anchor* when $S_0=1$.
- **topology** ([source](#decor:averages)): A topology is a finite rooted tree on labeled leaves, with unary vertices suppressed.
- **eta-regular depth assignment** ([source](#decor:averages)): A depth assignment is *$\eta$-regular* if every assigned depth divided by $L$ lies in $(\eta,1-\eta)$ and the normalized depths of distinct branching vertices differ by more than $\eta$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-first-moment | first moments of interaction amplitude and field | < | $\infty$ | fixed disorder laws |
| q-positivity | alternating moments of the factorization coefficient | ≥ | $0$ | every positive order |
| q-perturbation-count | perturbation total mean scale | = | $N^{3/4}$ | system size N |
| q-factor-bounds | single-site perturbation factor | ≤ | $3/2$ | each factor |
| q-perturbation-effect | effect of perturbations on total log partition function | ≤ | $(\log2)s_N$ | uniform in hierarchy, parameters and size |
| q-reservoir-mean | mean number of physical interactions in the reservoir | = | $\alpha(N+1)\left(\frac{N}{N+1}\right)^p$ | system size N |
| q-perturbation-parameter | perturbation parameter of type nu | = | $j^{-1}$ | type index j |
| q-charging-bound | sum of absolute extension coefficients | ≤ | $C_{q,k}\delta^b$ | constant independent of depth |
| q-reference-total | sum of coefficients for one constrained step | = | $m_d-jm_{d+1}$ | depth and number of excluded children |
| q-irregular-probability | probability of a non-regular tree | ≤ | $C_k(\eta+L^{-1})$ | fixed number of leaves |
| q-depth-continuity | averaged continuity of conditional means in depth | ≤ | $C/L$ | sufficiently large depth |
| q-projection-shift-error | averaged squared projection and shift error | ≤ | $C_k/L$ | fixed number of leaves |
| q-low-increment | cavity increment along the selected sequence | ≤ | $\underline F+2\varepsilon$ | every positive error |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-insertion** [claim](#stmt-tree-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-insertion): Backward induction over the power-mean recursion, then multiplication of edge density ratios. -> insertion shift and updated tree density
- **step-log-expansion** [claim](#stmt-tree-log-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-log-expansion): Repeated differentiation with protected leaves and normalization by the telescoping identity. -> series for the insertion logarithm
- **step-extension** [claim](#stmt-tree-extension-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-extension-expansion): Successive application of the differentiation rule with a protected anchor. -> mixed derivative expansion over extension histories
- **step-reference-total** [claim](#stmt-tree-reference-total) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-reference-total): Telescoping inside reference-free subtrees, then backward summation over choices. -> signed coefficient totals
- **step-charging** [claim](#stmt-tree-charging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-charging): Assign each target branching vertex to its creation step and bound step sums. -> absolute coefficient bound
- **step-regular-shapes** [claim](#stmt-tree-regular-shapes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-regular-shapes): Union bound over forbidden creation events in the tree growth rule. -> probability bounds for irregular trees and assignments
- **step-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-upper-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-bound): Interpolation, regularization of increments, then convexity of an even power with the positivity condition. -> upper bound on the pressure
- **step-cavity-increment** [claim](#stmt-cavity-increment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cavity-increment): Coupling of Poisson processes of consecutive sizes and discarding negligible terms. -> cavity increment identity
- **step-score-concentration** [claim](#stmt-pert-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pert-concentration): Convexity of the log partition function, Efron–Stein variance bound for the root, thermal variance bound. -> averaged concentration of each score
- **step-selection** [claim](#stmt-pert-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pert-selection): Averaging argument with Markov's inequality, then a diagonal subsequence. -> selected sizes and parameters
- **step-palm** [claim](#stmt-pert-palm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pert-palm): Poisson add-one identity applied to the score sum. -> score covariance equals analytic difference function
- **step-analytic** [claim](#stmt-pert-analytic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pert-analytic): Uniform analytic bounds, convergence of coefficients, and comparison with very small parameters. -> vanishing Taylor coefficients
- **step-tree-identity** [claim](#stmt-pert-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pert-identity): Polarization of the degree coefficient and selection of branching patterns by independent Rademacher marks. -> identities on prescribed colored trees
- **step-contractions** [claim](#stmt-decor-contractions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-decor-contractions): Cauchy–Schwarz and a trace inequality for a positive semidefinite covariance. -> contraction and three-copy estimates
- **step-depth-continuity** [claim](#stmt-decor-depth-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-decor-depth-continuity): Martingale energy bound and product difference inequality, one shifted vertex at a time. -> averaged continuity in depth
- **step-conditional-concentration** [claim](#stmt-decor-conditional-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-decor-conditional-concentration): Induction on the number of leaves using shifted copies, coefficient totals and a three-copy estimate. -> vanishing averaged conditional covariance
- **step-overlap-concentration** [claim](#stmt-decor-overlap-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-decor-conditional-concentration-2): Marker-anchor identity for the prefix mean, covariance control below the prefix, and continuity in depth. -> concentration of every multioverlap on depth average
- **step-encoding** [claim](#stmt-comp-encoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-encoding): Backward construction of conditional laws of the site magnetization along the hierarchy. -> deterministic trial law
- **step-patterns** [claim](#stmt-comp-patterns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-patterns): Expansion of the empirical pattern law in multioverlaps and telescoping for product measures. -> pattern comparison bound
- **step-insertion-comparison** [claim](#stmt-comp-insertion-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-insertion-comparison): Truncation of the Poisson count and of the logarithmic series, then comparison term by term. -> agreement of reservoir and trial increments
- **step-bounded-lower** [claim](#stmt-comp-bounded-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-bounded-lower): Each encoded trial value is at least the infimum; combine with the low increment and let the error vanish. -> lower bound for bounded laws
- **step-stability** [claim](#stmt-reduction-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reduction-stability): Pointwise coupling of Hamiltonians and preservation of bounds by power means. -> Lipschitz stability and shift identities
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reduction-stability-2): Scalar shift removing the multiplier, truncation, stability estimates, and the upper bound. -> existence of the limit and the variational formula
- **step-spin-model** [claim](#stmt-models-spin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-models-spin): Explicit factorization and verification of the hypotheses. -> formula for diluted even-spin models
- **step-sat-model** [claim](#stmt-models-sat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-models-sat): Explicit factorization of the soft clause weight and verification of the hypotheses. -> formula for soft even-arity satisfiability
- **step-ground-state** [claim](#stmt-models-ground) / [proof](#proof-stmt-models-ground): Sandwich of the pressure around the maximal energy and a zero-temperature limit. -> ground-state limit formula

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-limit-equals-infimum**: Under the stated integrability, factorization and positivity hypotheses, the limit of the pressure exists and equals the infimum of the hierarchical trial functional over all finite depths and trial laws.
- **inv-finite-size-upper-bound**: At every system size and for every admissible finite-level trial law, the pressure is bounded above by the trial functional.
- **inv-bounded-lower-bound**: For bounded permutation-invariant interaction laws and bounded field laws, the liminf of the pressure is at least the infimum of the trial functional over all depths.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-odd-arity-three-sat**: Apply the variational formula to random 3-SAT or other odd-arity clause models. -> The formula is proved only for even arity; for odd arity the trial functional differs and the proof does not apply.
- **anti-instance-level-certificate**: Treat the formula as a solver or finite-instance certificate for a single random instance. -> The result describes an expected limit over the ensemble and an infimum over hierarchies; it provides no search procedure and no finite-size certificate.
- **anti-outside-factorization-class**: Apply the equality to interaction laws that fail the factorization representation or the moment positivity condition. -> The upper bound by convexity needs factorization and positivity; without them the equality is not established by this argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an equality between a thermodynamic limit of expected log partition functions and an infimum over an infinite family of hierarchy depths and trial laws; it is not attained or computed by any finite procedure, and the supporting lemmas are analytic estimates in the limit of large system size and depth, so no finite constructive object with an executable check is stated.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-even-arity** (hypothesis): The variational equality is proved only for an even interaction arity, with Poisson connectivity of fixed density. Blocked use: ['Applying the formula to random 3-SAT or any odd-arity clause model', 'Applying the formula to dense or fixed-degree graph ensembles']
- **limit-first-moment** (hypothesis): The interaction amplitude and the field must have finite first moments; the disorder laws are fixed as the system size varies. Blocked use: ['Using the formula for heavy-tailed disorder without a finite first moment', 'Letting the disorder laws depend on the system size']
- **limit-factorization-positivity** (hypothesis): The upper bound needs the factorized representation of the exponentiated interaction and nonnegative alternating moments of the coefficient b. Blocked use: ['Applying the equality to interactions without the product factorization', 'Applying the equality to asymmetric couplings violating the moment positivity condition']
- **limit-infimum-not-attained** (nonconstructive): The right side is an infimum over finite hierarchies; the theorem does not assume that a trial law attains it or that an infinite-depth Gibbs state exists. Blocked use: ['Reading the infimum as a computable optimum with a terminating search', 'Assuming an optimal trial law exists']
- **limit-ensemble-level** (formal_scope): The result is a statement about the limit of the expected log partition function per spin, not about individual finite instances. Blocked use: ['Using it as a per-instance certificate', 'Using it as a solver or a search for satisfying assignments']
- **limit-selection-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The lower bound uses sizes and parameter vectors chosen by an averaging and diagonal subsequence argument, and the constants are not uniform in depth. Blocked use: ['Extracting an explicit rate of convergence in the system size', 'Treating the selected parameters as computable']
- **limit-ordered-limits** (approximation): Concentration and comparison hold only in ordered limits: first the system size at fixed depth, then the depth. Blocked use: ['Exchanging the order of the size and depth limits', 'Claiming uniform concentration in the depth']
- **limit-bounded-lower** (edge_case): The cavity lower bound is first proved for bounded permutation-invariant interactions and bounded fields, then extended by truncation. Blocked use: ['Applying the bounded lower bound directly to unbounded laws without the stability step']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 221 (`lean/docs/221.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Mézard–Parisi formula for diluted spin glasses](../../preprints/The-Mezard-Parisi-formula-for-diluted-spin-glasses-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the Mézard–Parisi hierarchical cavity formula for diluted even-arity Ising models in the Panchenko–Talagrand class. Under the class's factorization, independence, integrability, and positivity assumptions, the finite-system pressure converges to the infimum of the trial functional over all finite hierarchy depths and trial laws. The arity is any even integer at least two and the interaction density is positive.

Comparator source `DilutedSpin.lean` (6860 bytes, sha256 `457945ece56d8e51a5995d6cf6d44af979f0c5339adf7ea09abdbdf01473f6cf`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

 

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter
open scoped BigOperators ENNReal NNReal Topology

namespace DilutedSpinGlass

abbrev Spin := Bool

def spin (s : Spin) : ℝ := if s then 1 else -1

abbrev Interaction (p : ℕ) := (Fin p → Spin) → ℝ

/-- One interaction together with its factorization witness: theta,a,b,f. -/
abbrev InteractionSample (p : ℕ) :=
  Interaction p × ℝ × ℝ × (Fin p → Spin → ℝ)

structure Model (p : ℕ) where
  alpha : ℝ≥0
  disorder : ProbabilityMeasure (InteractionSample p)
  field : ProbabilityMeasure ℝ

/-- Exactly the standing hypotheses (model:integrability through positivity).
`norm` on the finite function space is max_s |theta(s)|. -/
structure Admissible {p : ℕ} (M : Model p) : Prop where
  arity : 2 ≤ p
  even : Even p
  density : 0 < M.alpha
  interaction_integrable : Integrable (fun z : InteractionSample p => ‖z.1‖)
    M.disorder.toMeasure
  field_integrable : Integrable (fun h : ℝ => |h|) M.field.toMeasure
  factorization : ∀ᵐ z ∂M.disorder.toMeasure,
    0 < z.2.1 ∧ ∀ s : Fin p → Spin,
      Real.exp (z.1 s) = z.2.1 * (1 + z.2.2.1 * ∏ l, z.2.2.2 l (s l)) ∧
      |z.2.2.1 * ∏ l, z.2.2.2 l (s l)| < 1
  independent_f : iIndepFun (fun l (z : InteractionSample p) => z.2.2.2 l)
    M.disorder.toMeasure
  identically_distributed_f : ∀ l j : Fin p,
    IdentDistrib (fun z : InteractionSample p => z.2.2.2 l)
      (fun z : InteractionSample p => z.2.2.2 j)
      M.disorder.toMeasure M.disorder.toMeasure
  independent_b : IndepFun (fun z : InteractionSample p => z.2.2.1)
    (fun z : InteractionSample p => z.2.2.2) M.disorder.toMeasure
  finite_b_moments : ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n →
    Integrable (fun z : InteractionSample p => (-z.2.2.1) ^ n) M.disorder.toMeasure
  positivity : ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n →
    0 ≤ ∫ z : InteractionSample p, (-z.2.2.1) ^ n ∂M.disorder.toMeasure

/-- Minus the Hamiltonian, with all index choices including repetitions. -/
noncomputable def logWeight {p N k : ℕ} (theta : Fin k → InteractionSample p)
    (h : Fin N → ℝ) (indices : Fin k → Fin p → Fin N) (σ : Fin N → Spin) : ℝ :=
  (∑ j, (theta j).1 (fun l => σ (indices j l))) + ∑ i, h i * spin (σ i)

noncomputable def logPartition {p N k : ℕ} (theta : Fin k → InteractionSample p)
    (h : Fin N → ℝ) (indices : Fin k → Fin p → Fin N) : ℝ :=
  Real.log (∑ σ : Fin N → Spin, Real.exp (logWeight theta h indices σ))

/-- F_N, using an explicit uniform average over the independent index labels.
The value at N=0 is irrelevant to the limit; valid system sizes are N>=1. -/
noncomputable def pressure {p : ℕ} (M : Model p) (N : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (∫ k : ℕ,
    ∫ theta : Fin k → InteractionSample p,
      ∫ h : Fin N → ℝ,
        ((∑ indices : Fin k → Fin p → Fin N, logPartition theta h indices) /
          (Fintype.card (Fin k → Fin p → Fin N) : ℝ))
      ∂Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin N => M.field.toMeasure)
    ∂Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin k => M.disorder.toMeasure)
  ∂poissonMeasure (M.alpha * N)) / N

noncomputable def q (x : ℝ) (s : Spin) : ℝ :=
  Real.exp (x * spin s) / (2 * Real.cosh x)

noncomputable def edge {p : ℕ} (theta : Interaction p) (x : Fin p → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ s : Fin p → Spin, Real.exp (theta s) * ∏ l, q (x l) (s l)

/-- Append the distinguished cavity spin in the last position. -/
def appendSpin {p : ℕ} (s : Fin (p - 1) → Spin) (ε : Spin) (l : Fin p) : Spin :=
  if h : l.val < p - 1 then s ⟨l.val, h⟩ else ε

noncomputable def message {p : ℕ} (theta : Interaction p)
    (x : Fin (p - 1) → ℝ) (ε : Spin) : ℝ :=
  Real.log (∑ s : Fin (p - 1) → Spin,
    Real.exp (theta (appendSpin s ε)) * ∏ l, q (x l) (s l))

noncomputable def siteLog {p k : ℕ} (theta : Fin k → InteractionSample p)
    (h : ℝ) (x : (Fin k × Fin (p - 1)) → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  Real.log ((∑ ε : Spin, Real.exp
    (h * spin ε + ∑ j, message (theta j).1 (fun l => x (j, l)) ε)) / 2)

/-- Use literally the Borel sigma-field of the weak topology at each level,
as stipulated in model.tex. -/
local instance instMeasurableSpaceCarrier_challenge (X : TopCat) : MeasurableSpace X := borel X
local instance instBorelSpaceCarrier_challenge (X : TopCat) : BorelSpace X := ⟨rfl⟩

/-- Nested probability laws: H_0=R and H_{j+1}=P(H_j), with weak topologies. -/
noncomputable def Hierarchy : ℕ → TopCat
  | 0 => TopCat.of ℝ
  | r + 1 => TopCat.of (ProbabilityMeasure (Hierarchy r))

/-- Logarithmic form of the conditional power means T_0 using independent
message labels. The current eta's are held fixed; only their descendants
are integrated. Physical disorder is a fixed argument of g throughout. -/
noncomputable def logMean {ι : Type} [Fintype ι] :
    (r : ℕ) → ((ι → ℝ) → ℝ) → (Fin r → ℝ) → (ι → Hierarchy r) → ℝ
  | 0, g, _, x => g x
  | r + 1, g, m, eta =>
    Real.log (∫ x : ι → Hierarchy r,
      Real.exp (m 0 * logMean r g (fun i => m i.succ) x)
      ∂Measure.pi (fun i => (eta i).toMeasure)) / m 0

/-- Expectation over the root laws eta_0 of each independent message label.
This also gives the r=0 convention (iid real messages, no power means). -/
noncomputable def trialLog {ι : Type} [Fintype ι] (r : ℕ)
    (ζ : Hierarchy (r + 1)) (m : Fin r → ℝ) (g : (ι → ℝ) → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∫ eta : ι → Hierarchy r, logMean r g m eta
    ∂Measure.pi (fun _ : ι => ζ.toMeasure)

/-- Admissible exponents 0<m_1<...<m_r<1; vacuous at r=0. -/
def Exponents {r : ℕ} (m : Fin r → ℝ) : Prop :=
  StrictMono m ∧ ∀ i, 0 < m i ∧ m i < 1

/-- The exact finite-depth cavity functional B_r. -/
noncomputable def functional {p : ℕ} (M : Model p) (r : ℕ)
    (ζ : Hierarchy (r + 1)) (m : Fin r → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  Real.log 2 +
    (∫ k : ℕ,
      ∫ theta : Fin k → InteractionSample p,
        ∫ h : ℝ, trialLog r ζ m (siteLog theta h) ∂M.field.toMeasure
      ∂Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin k => M.disorder.toMeasure)
    ∂poissonMeasure (M.alpha * p)) -
    (M.alpha : ℝ) * (p - 1 : ℕ) *
      (∫ theta : InteractionSample p,
        trialLog r ζ m (fun x => Real.log (edge theta.1 x)) ∂M.disorder.toMeasure)

noncomputable def phi {p : ℕ} (M : Model p) (r : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  sInf {v : ℝ | ∃ (ζ : Hierarchy (r + 1)) (m : Fin r → ℝ),
    Exponents m ∧ v = functional M r ζ m}

noncomputable def variationalValue {p : ℕ} (M : Model p) : ℝ :=
  ⨅ r : ℕ, phi M r

/-- main:theorem. No boundedness of interactions, fields, or messages is added.
Existence of the thermodynamic limit is part of this convergence assertion. -/
theorem mezard_parisi {p : ℕ} (M : Model p) (hM : Admissible M) :
    Tendsto (pressure M) atTop (𝓝 (variationalValue M)) := by
  sorry

end DilutedSpinGlass

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Mézard–Parisi hierarchical cavity formula for diluted even-arity Ising models in the Panchenko–Talagrand class. This resolves the variational equality conjecture for that class: the limiting pressure equals the infimum of the trial functional over all finite hierarchy depths and trial laws. Only first moments of the interaction and external field are required.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-main-theorem](#stmt-main-theorem) | theorem | `main:theorem` | 1 | Under [model:integrability]–[model:positivity], the thermodynamic limit exists and |
| [stmt-tree-insertion](#stmt-tree-insertion) | lemma | `tree:insertion` | 2 | Suppose a leaf partition function is iterated by power means, with exponents $m_1,\ldots,m_{L-1}$, and $K_1,\ldots,K_{L-1}$ are its tilted kernels. |
| [stmt-tree-log-expansion](#stmt-tree-log-expansion) | lemma | `tree:log-expansion` | 3 | For a bounded real path function $D$ and $A=1+tD$, |
| [stmt-tree-extension-expansion](#stmt-tree-extension-expansion) | lemma | `tree:extension-expansion` | 4 | Let $H$ be a bounded function of the variables on the old tree, fixed as the insertion parameters vary. |
| [stmt-tree-reference-total](#stmt-tree-reference-total) | lemma | `tree:reference-total` | 5 | Suppose the next color must follow a specified reference prefix to depth $d$ and then enter a child distinct from all $j\geq1$ children there that contain reference paths. |

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

A diluted spin glass has a finite mean number of interactions per spin. Its local environment remains random as the system grows, and the cavity method describes that environment through a distribution of local fields. When many competing states contribute to the partition function, the proposed order parameter is a hierarchy of distributions of these fields. The thermodynamic question is whether this hierarchy determines the limiting expected log partition function per spin. We call this quantity *pressure*. At inverse temperature $\beta$, the usual physical free energy per spin is its negative divided by $\beta$.

The Viana–Bray model is a classical diluted two-spin model (Viana and Bray 1985) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[VB85](#ref-VB85)]. Mézard and Parisi (Mézard and Parisi 2001) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[MP01](#ref-MP01)] developed the hierarchical cavity picture for the fixed-connectivity Bethe-lattice spin glass, with an explicit one-step replica-symmetry-breaking functional. Franz and Leone (Franz and Leone 2003) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[FL03](#ref-FL03)] adapted Guerra’s interpolation method (Guerra 2003) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Guerra03](#ref-Guerra03)] to diluted systems and obtained replica-symmetric and one-step pressure bounds for even-arity models. Panchenko and Talagrand (Panchenko and Talagrand 2004) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[PT04](#ref-PT04)] then proved the arbitrary finite-level hierarchical upper bounds considered here.

We prove that the infimum of these bounds is the limiting pressure. This resolves the equality conjecture stated immediately after Theorem 4 of Panchenko–Talagrand (Panchenko and Talagrand 2004) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[PT04](#ref-PT04)], for their Poisson, even-arity Ising model class with factorized interactions and the specified positivity condition. Only first moments of the interaction and external field are required. The result includes the Viana–Bray model, diluted even-spin models with symmetric couplings, and soft random even-$K$ SAT at every positive temperature. The exact model and trial functional are defined in <a href="#model:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:section">2</a>; the named models and their expected ground-state limits are treated in <a href="#models:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="models:section">10</a>.

An exact variational description and reduction to a prescribed trial class are distinct problems. The cavity variational principle of Aizenman, Sims and Starr (Aizenman, Sims, and Starr 2003) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ASS03](#ref-ASS03)] for the Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model has a Viana–Bray counterpart in De Sanctis’s formulation over random multioverlap structures (De Sanctis 2004) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DeSanctis04](#ref-DeSanctis04)]. Panchenko’s spin-distribution approach (Panchenko 2013, Theorems 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Pan13](#ref-Pan13)] gives an exact variational formula over invariant exchangeable spin distributions. Under their positivity condition, Coja-Oghlan and Perkins (Coja-Oghlan and Perkins 2019) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[COP19](#ref-COP19)] obtain an invariant-kernel variational formula for random regular factor graphs. These results use different descriptions of the state from the finite hierarchy of message laws optimized here. The additional task is to recover the specific hierarchical cavity functional without losing the pressure.

Panchenko (Panchenko 2016, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Pan16](#ref-Pan16)] established a hierarchical single-site representation for finite-RSB asymptotic Gibbs measures of suitably modified models; approximation of general states by that class remains a separate step. Biswas, Chen and Sen (Biswas, Chen, and Sen 2025, Theorem 1.1 and Proposition 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BCS25](#ref-BCS25)] obtain exact replica-symmetric pressure formulas in high-temperature and critical or subcritical regimes for compact real spin spaces under moment and regularity assumptions. For the zero-field Ising spin glass with Rademacher couplings on random regular graphs, Concetti (Concetti 2025, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Concetti25](#ref-Concetti25)] gives a full-replica-symmetry-breaking pressure bound no larger than each optimized finite-level bound; equality with that functional is conjectural in that work. The result here identifies the finite-depth Panchenko–Talagrand infimum at arbitrary density in its stated interaction class.

The main obstacle is the distribution of spins seen across several replicas, that is, several samples from the same Gibbs system. The probability of each replica-spin pattern depends on products of every subset of those spins. The spatial average of such a product is a *multioverlap*; pair overlaps alone do not determine all these patterns. This is the information isolated by the spin-distribution approach (Panchenko 2013) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Pan13](#ref-Pan13)]. To compare a cavity insertion with independent hierarchical messages, we must control all finite multioverlaps before redrawing the message roots independently.

We work with finite auxiliary hierarchies before taking the thermodynamic limit. Their branching is part of the construction; no ultrametric organization of the physical Gibbs states is assumed. Bounded perturbations yield identities for prescribed replica branching patterns. Delaying each branching depth by one level permits an induction that concentrates multioverlaps *on average over branching depths*. The cavity insertion itself has an expansion averaging over finite trees, so this averaged control is sufficient. The system size tends to infinity at each fixed depth; only then does the depth increase. The proof strategy following <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> explains how the finite-tree calculus, concentration and independent-message comparison fit together.

<a id="model:section"></a>
### The model and the variational principle

<a id="model:integrability"></a>
<a id="model:hamiltonian"></a>
Fix an even integer $p\ge2$ and $\alpha>0$. Let $\theta$ be a random real function on $\{-1,1\}^p$ and let $h$ be a real random variable. Write $B(\theta)=\max_s\lvert\theta(s)\rvert$ and assume $$\label{model:integrability}
 \mathbb EB(\theta)+\mathbb E\lvert h\rvert<\infty.$$ For independent $M_N\sim\mathop{\mathrm{Poisson}}(\alpha N)$, independent copies $\theta_k$ of $\theta$, independent copies $h_i$ of $h$, and independent uniform indices $i_{\ell,k}\in[N]=\{1,\ldots,N\}$, set $$\label{model:hamiltonian}
 \begin{gathered}
 -H_N(\sigma)=\sum_{k=1}^{M_N}
 \theta_k(\sigma_{i_{1,k}},\ldots,\sigma_{i_{p,k}})
 +\sum_{i=1}^N h_i\sigma_i,\\
 Z_N=\sum_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^N}e^{-H_N(\sigma)},
 \qquad F_N=\frac1N\mathbb E\log Z_N.
 \end{gathered}$$ All families in this definition are mutually independent. In particular, indices are sampled with replacement. The model parameters and disorder laws are fixed as $N$ varies.

<a id="model:factorization"></a>
<a id="model:positivity"></a>
The interaction hypotheses are the existence of a representation $$\label{model:factorization}
 e^{\theta(s_1,\ldots,s_p)}
 =a\left(1+b\prod_{\ell=1}^p f_\ell(s_\ell)\right),
 \qquad
 \lvert b\prod_{\ell=1}^p f_\ell(s_\ell)\rvert<1\quad\text{a.s.},$$ for every spin tuple, where $a>0$ and $b$ are real random variables, the $f_\ell:\{-1,1\}\to\mathbb R$ are independent copies of one random function, $b$ is independent of them, and $$\label{model:positivity}
 \mathbb E[(-b)^n]\ge0\qquad(n\ge1).$$ The expectations in <a href="#model:positivity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:positivity">[model:positivity]</a> are interpreted as finite real expectations. This is automatic in the nondegenerate case: independence and the strict product bound force $b$ and $\max_s|f_1(s)|$ to be essentially bounded, as shown in <a href="#upper:bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="upper:bound">9</a>. If the product term vanishes identically, the interaction is spin independent and one may take $b=0$. The multiplier $a$ may depend on all the other variables in the representation; no separate integrability of $\log a$ is assumed.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The hierarchical cavity functional

We give the power-mean form of the Panchenko–Talagrand functional (Panchenko and Talagrand 2004, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[PT04](#ref-PT04)]. A message $x$ specifies a probability distribution on one spin. The site term inserts a new spin with its incident interactions; the edge term corrects for interactions counted in that insertion.

For $x\in\mathbb R$ let $q_x(s)=e^{xs}/(2\cosh x)$. Define <a id="model:edge"></a><a id="model:site-message"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 C_\theta(x_1,\ldots,x_p)
 &=\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^p}e^{\theta(s)}\prod_{\ell=1}^p q_{x_\ell}(s_\ell),
 \label{model:edge}\\
 U_\theta(x_1,\ldots,x_{p-1};\varepsilon)
 &=\log\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{p-1}}
 e^{\theta(s_1,\ldots,s_{p-1},\varepsilon)}
 \prod_{\ell=1}^{p-1}q_{x_\ell}(s_\ell).
 \label{model:site-message}
\end{aligned}$$ Both $\lvert\log C_\theta\rvert$ and $\lvert U_\theta\rvert$ are bounded by $B(\theta)$, uniformly in the messages.

<a id="model:chain"></a>
<a id="model:vs"></a>
<a id="model:functional"></a>
Write $\mathcal P(E)$ for the Borel probability measures on $E$. Let $\mathcal M_1=\mathcal P(\mathbb R)$ and $\mathcal M_{j+1}=\mathcal P(\mathcal M_j)$, equipped with the topology of weak convergence and its Borel sigma-field. Fix $r\ge1$, $\zeta\in\mathcal M_{r+1}$, and $0<m_1<\cdots<m_r<1$. For each message label $\omega$ independently, draw $$\label{model:chain}
 \eta_0^\omega\sim\zeta,\qquad
 \eta_{d+1}^\omega\mid\eta_d^\omega\sim\eta_d^\omega\ (0\le d\le r-2),
 \qquad x^\omega\mid\eta_{r-1}^\omega\sim\eta_{r-1}^\omega.$$ At $r=1$, this means that each label first draws a random probability measure $\eta_0$ with law $\zeta$, then draws $x$ with law $\eta_0$. Higher levels repeat this sampling of measures. All these variables are independent of the physical disorder. Use different labels for $x_1,\ldots,x_p$ and for $x_\ell^j$, $j\ge1$, $1\le\ell<p$. Take independent $k\sim\mathop{\mathrm{Poisson}}(\alpha p)$, $\theta,\theta_1,\ldots$, and $h$, and put <a id="model:ve"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 V_{\rm s}&=\frac12\sum_{\varepsilon=\pm1}
 \exp\left(h\varepsilon+\sum_{j=1}^k
 U_{\theta_j}(x_1^j,\ldots,x_{p-1}^j;\varepsilon)\right),\label{model:vs}\\
 V_{\rm e}&=C_\theta(x_1,\ldots,x_p).\label{model:ve}
\end{aligned}$$ For $0\le d<r$, let $\mathscr F_d$ be generated by all the physical disorder in these expressions and all $\eta_j^\omega$, $0\le j\le d$. Set <a id="model:power-means"></a>$$\label{model:power-means}
 T_rV=V,\qquad
 T_dV=\left(\mathbb E[(T_{d+1}V)^{m_{d+1}}\mid\mathscr F_d]\right)^{1/m_{d+1}}
 \quad(d=r-1,\ldots,0).$$ Only auxiliary messages are integrated in these conditional expectations. For example, at depth one, $T_0V=(\mathbb E_x[V^{m_1}\mid\boldsymbol\eta_0])^{1/m_1}$. At depth two, each label draws $\eta_0\sim\zeta$, then $\eta_1\mid\eta_0\sim\eta_0$, and then $x\mid\eta_1\sim\eta_1$, and $$T_0V=\left(\mathbb E_{\boldsymbol\eta_1}
   \left[\left(\mathbb E_{\boldsymbol x}
      [V^{m_2}\mid\boldsymbol\eta_1]\right)^{m_1/m_2}
      \,\middle|\,\boldsymbol\eta_0\right]\right)^{1/m_1}.$$ Here bold symbols collect all message labels in $V$; physical disorder is held fixed throughout, including in the two abbreviated displays. Define $$\label{model:functional}
 \mathcal B_r(\zeta,\mathbf m)
 =\log2+\mathbb E\log T_0V_{\rm s}
 -\alpha(p-1)\mathbb E\log T_0V_{\rm e},
 \qquad \Phi_r=\inf_{\zeta,\,\mathbf m}\mathcal B_r(\zeta,\mathbf m).$$ At $r=0$, the messages are independent with common law $\zeta\in\mathcal P(\mathbb R)$ and the operators $T_d$ are omitted; this defines $\mathcal B_0(\zeta)$ and $\Phi_0$.

<a id="model:trial-bounds"></a>
The quantities in <a href="#model:functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:functional">[model:functional]</a> are finite without any moment assumption on the trial messages. Indeed, the power-mean operators preserve pointwise multiplicative bounds, and $$\label{model:trial-bounds}
 \lvert\log T_0V_{\rm s}\rvert\le\lvert h\rvert+\sum_{j=1}^k B(\theta_j),
 \qquad \lvert\log T_0V_{\rm e}\rvert\le B(\theta).$$

<a id="stmt-main-theorem"></a>
<div id="main:theorem" class="theorem">

<a id="main:formula"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Under <a href="#model:integrability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:integrability">[model:integrability]</a>–<a href="#model:positivity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:positivity">[model:positivity]</a>, the thermodynamic limit exists and $$\label{main:formula}
 \lim_{N\to\infty}F_N=\inf_{r\ge0}\Phi_r.$$*

</div>

The right side is an infimum over finite hierarchies. The theorem does not assume that a trial law attains it or that a limiting infinite-depth Gibbs state has been constructed.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy

The interpolation upper bound holds at every system size and every finite depth. We reproduce it in <a href="#upper:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="upper:section">4</a>, including a normalization that avoids assuming integrability of the multiplier $\log a$. The lower bound has four steps.

1.  *Select a cavity reservoir.* A reservoir is a common Gibbs system on $N$ spins used to compare systems of sizes $N$ and $N+1$. For bounded interaction and field laws, add bounded Poisson perturbations whose number is sublinear in the system size. Their effect on the pressure vanishes. At each fixed hierarchy depth, select system sizes and deterministic perturbation parameters for which the difference between successive expected logarithms after the auxiliary power-mean recursion is at most an arbitrarily small error above the original pressure liminf. The same choices give concentration of every perturbation score ([cavity:section,pert:selection]).

2.  *Prescribe branching patterns.* An elementary calculus for finite probability trees expands insertions into signed sums over added replica paths. Independent marks select any prescribed branching pattern. Score concentration then gives identities for these signed sums ([tree:section,pert:tree-identity] [tree:section](#tree:section), [pert:tree-identity](#pert:tree-identity)).

3.  *Concentrate all multioverlaps.* Move each internal branching depth of a target tree one step later. The first fresh split at each new depth supplies the same small factor as the normalizing coefficient. A relative bound on the sum of absolute coefficients preserves control of errors after division. Conditional covariance then decays by induction on the number of replicas. A second identity concentrates the remaining conditional mean (<a href="#decor:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="decor:section">7</a>).

4.  *Recover independent cavity messages.* Encode a uniformly chosen reservoir site’s entire finite hierarchy by successive conditional laws. Multioverlap concentration permits different site labels to be replaced by independent copies of this process in the cavity insertion. This yields an admissible trial value close to the selected increment (<a href="#comp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:section">8</a>).

The estimates in Step 3 are averages over separated interior branching depths. The insertion expansion assigns vanishing probability to the exceptional trees, so these averages are sufficient for Step 4. No uniform concentration in the depth is needed. The resulting lower bound holds for every bounded interaction law invariant under permutations of its inputs, without factorization or positivity. Finally, normalization and estimates uniform over all trial laws remove boundedness and give <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> under the stated first moment assumption (<a href="#reduction:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="reduction:section">9</a>).

<a id="tree:section"></a>
### Calculus on finite trees

A power-mean insertion admits two expansions. The logarithmic expansion will compare pressures; an expansion with one canceled replica factor will extract the perturbation identities. Both follow from one differentiation rule on trees. We also bound their coefficients and the probability of exceptional branching depths. Power-mean calculations for hierarchical trial laws appear in Mézard–Parisi (Mézard and Parisi 2001, Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MP01](#ref-MP01)] and Panchenko–Talagrand (Panchenko and Talagrand 2004, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[PT04](#ref-PT04)]; the latter uses cascade weights introduced by Ruelle (Ruelle 1987) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Ruelle87](#ref-Ruelle87)]. Here we derive the needed identities directly for finite sequences of arbitrary probability kernels, without assuming an infinite hierarchy.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Path kernels and insertions

Fix an integer $L\geq1$ and numbers $$0=m_0<m_1<\cdots<m_{L-1}<m_L=1.$$ A path has depths $0,\ldots,L$. Its depth-zero variable is the root. For $1\leq d\leq L$, the probability kernel $K_d$ draws the next variable given the whole prefix through depth $d-1$; $\mathscr F_d$ is the prefix sigma-field. The root distribution is fixed throughout all insertions. In an $r$-level application, $L=r+1$: depths $1,\ldots,r$ carry auxiliary variables, and depth $L$ carries a Gibbs spin configuration. For the trial law <a href="#model:chain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:chain">[model:chain]</a> with $r\ge1$, $\eta_d$ is the depth-$d$ variable for $0\le d<r$, and $x$ is the depth-$r$ variable.

A finite tree $\mathcal T$ consists of a common root and finitely many labeled paths, each ending at depth $L$. Every internal vertex has at least one child; in particular, all unary portions of paths are retained. At each vertex, different children are drawn independently using the appropriate kernel. We write $E_{\mathcal T}$ for this sampling expectation conditional on the root. A split at depth $d$ means that the last common vertex is at depth $d$. Replica positions at depth $L$ are distinct vertices, but the values sampled there need not differ. Thus independent spin replicas may be the same spin configuration. Removing some leaves and the portions of their paths unused by the remaining leaves preserves the sampling law of the remaining tree.

<a id="tree:multioverlap"></a>
When the terminal variables are spin configurations $\sigma^a\in\{-1,1\}^N$, the multioverlap on a subset $S$ of the replica leaves is $$\label{tree:multioverlap}
 R_S=\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\prod_{a\in S}\sigma_i^a,
 \qquad R_\varnothing=1.$$ An unspecified expectation $\mathbb E$ below includes the root and the sampling of the indicated tree.

Here is how the path kernels arise from a partition function. Let $P_d$, $1\le d<L$, be the auxiliary prior kernels, put $X_{L-1}=\log Z$, and recursively set $$X_{d-1}=m_d^{-1}\log P_d e^{m_dX_d}\qquad(1\le d<L).$$ The corresponding *tilted kernels* are <a id="tree:original-tilt"></a>$$\label{tree:original-tilt}
 K_d(dz\mid\mathscr F_{d-1})
 =e^{m_d(X_d(z)-X_{d-1})}P_d(dz\mid\mathscr F_{d-1}),
 \qquad 1\le d<L.$$ They are probability kernels by the recursion. The final kernel $K_L$ is the Gibbs distribution of the spin configuration conditional on its auxiliary prefix. When $L=1$, only this final kernel is present.

<a id="tree:Y-recursion"></a>
<a id="tree:updated-kernel"></a>
For a positive path function $A$, define $$\label{tree:Y-recursion}
  Y_L=A,\qquad
  Y_{d-1}=\bigl(K_dY_d^{m_d}\bigr)^{1/m_d},
  \qquad d=L,\ldots,1.$$ We initially suppose that $A$ is bounded above and away from zero, conditionally on the root. Every expression below is then well defined. Denote the kernels after insertion by $$\label{tree:updated-kernel}
  K_d^A(dz\mid\mathscr F_{d-1})
  =\left(\frac{Y_d(z)}{Y_{d-1}}\right)^{m_d}
     K_d(dz\mid\mathscr F_{d-1}).$$ The recursion shows that these are probability kernels. Superscript $A$ on a tree expectation means that these kernels are used.

<a id="stmt-tree-insertion"></a>
<div id="tree:insertion" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Insertion calculus). *Suppose a leaf partition function is iterated by power means, with exponents $m_1,\ldots,m_{L-1}$, and $K_1,\ldots,K_{L-1}$ are its tilted kernels. Let $K_L$ be its terminal Gibbs kernel. Multiplying each spin weight by $A$ changes the iterated root logarithm by $\log Y_0$, where <a href="#tree:Y-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:Y-recursion">[tree:Y-recursion]</a> uses the kernels before insertion. The new kernels are <a href="#tree:updated-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:updated-kernel">[tree:updated-kernel]</a>.*

<a id="tree:density"></a>
*For an internal vertex $\beta$ of a prescribed tree, let $d_\beta$ be its depth, let $n_\beta$ be its number of children, and put $$\gamma_\beta=m_{d_\beta}-n_\beta m_{d_\beta+1}.$$ The conditional joint density of its updated tree law is $$\label{tree:density}
  W_{\mathcal T}(A)
  :=\frac{dP_{\mathcal T}^A}{dP_{\mathcal T}}
  =\prod_{a\in\operatorname{Leaves}(\mathcal T)}A^a
       \prod_{\beta\in\operatorname{Int}(\mathcal T)}
            Y_\beta^{\gamma_\beta}.$$ Here $Y_\beta$ is $Y_{d_\beta}$ evaluated on the prefix at $\beta$.*

*The recursion is monotone and homogeneous: if $a\leq A\leq b$, for positive root-measurable $a,b$, then $a\leq Y_d\leq b$ at every depth; and multiplying $A$ by a positive root-measurable constant multiplies every $Y_d$ by that constant.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-tree-insertion"><summary>Proof of stmt-tree-insertion</summary>

*Proof.* At the terminal spin integration, the partition-function ratio is $K_LA=Y_{L-1}$, because $m_L=1$. If $X_d$ and $X_d^A$ are the old and new backward logarithms, substitution in the preceding power mean gives $$\exp\bigl(m_d(X_{d-1}^A-X_{d-1})\bigr)
  =K_d\exp\bigl(m_d(X_d^A-X_d)\bigr).$$ Backward induction proves $X_d^A-X_d=\log Y_d$ and then gives <a href="#tree:updated-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:updated-kernel">[tree:updated-kernel]</a>; the same formula at $d=L$ is the usual Gibbs density ratio.

Multiply the edge density ratios in <a href="#tree:updated-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:updated-kernel">[tree:updated-kernel]</a> over the tree. An internal vertex at positive depth $d$ receives exponent $m_d$ from its incoming edge and exponent $-n_\beta m_{d+1}$ from its outgoing edges. At the root there is no incoming edge, which agrees with $m_0=0$. Each terminal vertex receives exponent $m_L=1$. This proves <a href="#tree:density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:density">[tree:density]</a>. Monotonicity and homogeneity follow at once by backward induction in <a href="#tree:Y-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:Y-recursion">[tree:Y-recursion]</a>. ◻

</details>

An insertion may use additional variables that were absent from the original model. Enlarge each path kernel by their independent prior kernels before applying the lemma. The original backward logarithms do not depend on these variables, so the original tilt leaves their conditional prior kernels unchanged. Additional root variables remain quenched.

<a id="tree:first-variation"></a>
We will use differentiation of <a href="#tree:Y-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:Y-recursion">[tree:Y-recursion]</a>.
