

<a id="decor:relative-absolute-cost"></a>
Let $\kappa(T)$ be the reference coefficient <a href="#tree:K" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:K">[tree:K]</a>, and put <a id="decor:J-definition"></a>$$J=\kappa(S^+)^2.
 \label{decor:J-definition}$$ At each vertex the $j=1$ factor is $-\delta$; for $j\ge2$ its absolute value is at least $\eta/2$ for all sufficiently large $L$. If $b=b(S)$, then <a id="decor:J-lower-bound"></a>$$|J|\ge c_{k,\eta}\delta^{2b}>0.
 \label{decor:J-lower-bound}$$ For large $L$, regularity ensures that each shifted depth differs from every branching depth of the old tree, including $d$. Thus these $2b$ target branching vertices must be created by fresh unary splits. Lemma <a href="#tree:charging" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tree:charging">6</a> gives $$\frac1{|J|}\sum_{\text{allowed extensions}}|c|
        \le C_{k,\eta}.
 \label{decor:relative-absolute-cost}$$ The two copies have aligned depths but distinct branching vertices: each vertex supplies its own factor $\delta$. This is the reason for the shift. The full signed coefficient has an additional factor $-\delta$ from separation at $d$; we divide by $J$ and leave that factor in the identity. Equation <a href="#decor:relative-absolute-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:relative-absolute-cost">[decor:relative-absolute-cost]</a> then controls every projection error after division.

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
###### Projecting the two target sides.

<a id="decor:projection-shift-error"></a>
In every extension, the marginal target tree consists of two ordinary copies of $S^+$, independent below their separation at $d$. By <a href="#decor:projection-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:projection-bound">[decor:projection-bound]</a> its multioverlap differs in $L^2$ from the contraction of its projected means at $e+1$ by at most $2p_N(S^+,e+1)$. Change the proper child depths back by one, then change their evaluation depth from $e+1$ to $e$. The product difference inequality and Lemma <a href="#decor:depth-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="decor:depth-continuity">19</a> show that this adds an error at most $h_N(S)$ in $L^2$, with $$\mathcal A_{L,\eta}h_N(S)^2\le C_k/L.
 \label{decor:projection-shift-error}$$ One can take $h_N(S)$ to be the sum of the finitely many $L^2(\|\cdot\|_N)$ distances in these comparisons, multiplied by a size-dependent constant. This definition depends only on the single-copy marginal shapes, not on the particular extension. Thus <a href="#decor:projection-shift-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:projection-shift-error">[decor:projection-shift-error]</a> holds uniformly for the comparisons in the extension sum, even though the union of the old and target trees itself need not have regular depths.

Let $a,b$ denote the vectors $a_{S,e}$ on the old stems I and II. The target stem in I shares the old prefix through $e$ because it contains the anchor, so its final projected vector is $a$.

<a id="pm-h-34"></a>
###### The signed extension sum.

Set $f$ to be the overlap of all $2k$ old leaves, an allowed fixed overlap monomial. All target-I additions stay in the anchor’s branch below $d$, so they leave the old-II stem and its child counts unchanged. Classify the first added path of target side II by its prefix through $e$. It may diverge freshly at $\ell=d$, with coefficient $-c_d$, or follow the old II stem and diverge at $d<\ell<e$, with coefficient $-c_\ell=-\delta$. In such a class let $b_\ell$ be $a_{S,e}$ on the new stem; it shares the old II prefix through $\ell$. At $\ell=d$ the stems carrying $a,b,b_d$ are conditionally independent below $d$. Every remaining possibility follows the old II prefix through $e$ and has final projected vector $b$. All later target paths in side II follow its first path through $e$, since their earliest split is $e+1$.

For a fixed extension history, retain every old path and the first target-II prefix through $e$, and delete all other new branches. The target-I projection $a$ already depends only on the retained old anchor prefix. Each deleted subtree integrates to one under its probability kernels; a reused old leaf introduces no new variable. The retained tree depends only on the class: it is the old tree with a fresh stem attached at $\ell<e$, or just the old tree in the remaining class. The history is a combinatorial choice, not a condition on sampled values. Thus the joint marginal of the old paths and projected prefixes is fixed within each class. In particular, the expectation of $f$ times the projected overlap is constant within a class and can be multiplied by its total signed coefficient.

By Lemma <a href="#tree:reference-total" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tree:reference-total">5</a>, summing later side-II colors backwards gives $\kappa(S^+)$ for each first-path choice. Summing the side-I choices gives the other factor $\kappa(S^+)$. The fresh class at $\ell$ therefore has total coefficient $-c_\ell J$. The total coefficient is $-\delta J$, both from the old tree and from the anchor alone. Since $$\sum_{\ell=d}^{e-1}c_\ell=(e+1)\delta=m_e+\delta,$$ the remaining prefix-through-$e$ class has coefficient $m_eJ$. Thus the projected terms have the following coefficients: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{side-II prefix} & \text{projected overlap}
      & \text{total coefficient}\\ \hline
 \text{fresh divergence at }d\le\ell<e & a\cdot b_\ell & -c_\ell J\\
 \text{old prefix through }e & a\cdot b & m_eJ
 \end{array}$$

<a id="decor:induction-identity"></a>
Multiply the projected overlap in each row by $f$ and divide the prescribed-tree identity by $J$. Rearranging gives $$\sum_{\ell=d}^{e-1}c_\ell
   \mathbb E\bigl[f(a\cdot b-a\cdot b_\ell)\bigr]
 =\delta\bigl(\mathbb E[f(a\cdot b)]
          -\mathbb Ef\,\mathbb ER_{\mathrm{target}}\bigr)
       +\rho_N(S,d),
 \label{decor:induction-identity}$$ where $R_{\mathrm{target}}$ on the right is the target overlap in the anchor-alone extension. Its absolute value is at most one. The error satisfies <a id="decor:induction-error"></a>$$|\rho_N(S,d)|\le C_{k,\eta}
       \bigl(p_N(S^+,e+1)+h_N(S)\bigr)
       +C_{L,k,\eta}\varepsilon_{N,L,k}.
 \label{decor:induction-error}$$ Indeed, multiplying a projection error by $f$, even when correlated with it, costs at most its $L^2$ norm since $|f|\le1$. Sum these errors with absolute coefficients and apply <a href="#decor:relative-absolute-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:relative-absolute-cost">[decor:relative-absolute-cost]</a>. The final term includes division of the original identity error by $J$ and tends to zero at fixed $L$.

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
###### Closing the induction.

<a id="decor:projected-three-copy"></a>
<a id="decor:fiber-bound"></a>
Equation <a href="#decor:projection-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:projection-bound">[decor:projection-bound]</a> gives $\|f-a\cdot b\|_{L^2}\le2p_N(S,e)$. Moreover $\sum_{\ell=d}^{e-1}c_\ell=(e+1)\delta\le1$. Replacing $f$ by $a\cdot b$ on the left of <a href="#decor:induction-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:induction-identity">[decor:induction-identity]</a> therefore costs at most $4p_N(S,e)$. Each $a\cdot b_\ell$ has the same marginal law as $a\cdot b$, so that left side becomes $$\frac12\sum_{\ell=d}^{e-1}c_\ell
        \mathbb E(a\cdot b-a\cdot b_\ell)^2.$$ Its summands are nonnegative and $c_d\ge\eta$. The right side of <a href="#decor:induction-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:induction-identity">[decor:induction-identity]</a> is bounded in absolute value by $2\delta+|\rho_N(S,d)|$. Hence $$\mathbb E(a\cdot b-a\cdot b_d)^2
 \le C_\eta\bigl(\delta+p_N(S,e)+|\rho_N(S,d)|\bigr).
 \label{decor:projected-three-copy}$$ Sample three copies of $A_S$ conditionally independently below $d$. Projecting each of the two contractions involving the first copy changes it in $L^2$ by at most $2p_N(S,e)$. Using <a href="#decor:three-copy-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:three-copy-bound">[decor:three-copy-bound]</a>, <a href="#decor:projected-three-copy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:projected-three-copy">[decor:projected-three-copy]</a>, and $p_N(S,e)\le k$, we obtain <a id="decor:covariance-induction-estimate"></a>$$v_N(S,d)\le C_{k,\eta}\bigl(
       \delta+p_N(S,e)+p_N(S^+,e+1)+h_N(S)\bigr)
       +C_{L,k,\eta}\varepsilon_{N,L,k}.
 \label{decor:covariance-induction-estimate}$$ Each child in either $p_N$ has fewer than $k$ leaves. To make the averaging step explicit, the aligned construction has $D=b(S)+1$ distinct depth coordinates, including $d$ and $e$. A term belonging to $T_j$ uses only $D_j=b(T_j)+1$ coordinates. For the shifted term these are $\pi_j(\mathbf q)=(e+1,(q_v+1)_{v\in\operatorname{Br}(T_j)})$. Every retained assignment has at most $L^{D-D_j}$ preimages, and its coordinates are $\eta/2$-regular for large $L$. If $w_j$ is the child covariance as a function of these coordinates, then, for $0<\gamma\le1$, $$\begin{aligned}
 L^{-D}\sum_{\mathbf q\;\eta\text{-regular}}
       w_j(\pi_j(\mathbf q))^\gamma
 &\le L^{-D_j}\sum_{\mathbf y\;\eta/2\text{-regular}}w_j(\mathbf y)^\gamma
 \notag\\
 &\le\left(L^{-D_j}\sum_{\mathbf y\;\eta/2\text{-regular}}
       w_j(\mathbf y)\right)^\gamma.
 \label{decor:fiber-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ The unshifted term has the same bound without translating the coordinates. Thus the two aligned copies use single-copy estimates; there is no restriction of an independently averaged pair of copies to a diagonal. Taking $\gamma=1/4$ and then $\limsup_N$, and using <a href="#decor:projection-shift-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:projection-shift-error">[decor:projection-shift-error]</a> and <a href="#decor:identity-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:identity-error">[decor:identity-error]</a>, gives, for sufficiently large $L$, <a id="decor:induction-recursion"></a>$$V_{k,L}(\eta)\le C_{k,\eta}\left(
       L^{-1/2}+V_{k-1,L}(\eta/2)^{1/4}\right).
 \label{decor:induction-recursion}$$ The maximum over smaller topologies in <a href="#decor:V-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:V-definition">[decor:V-definition]</a> can be absorbed in the right side because these covariances are bounded by one. The base case and induction now imply $V_{k,L}(\eta)\to0$ for every fixed $k,\eta$, proving the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
#### Concentration of every multioverlap

The covariance estimate controls fluctuations below a sampled prefix. We now use a marker anchor to concentrate the prefix projection, and combine this with the averaged continuity estimate.

<details id="proof-stmt-decor-conditional-concentration-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-decor-conditional-concentration</summary>

*Proof of <a href="#decor:overlap-concentration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="decor:overlap-concentration">17</a>.* For a singleton, use the spin-anchor identity with no added colors and $f=R_S$. It gives $\mathbb ER_S^2=(\mathbb ER_S)^2+o_N(1)$.

<a id="decor:prefix-projection-concentration"></a>
Suppose $S$ first splits at $e$ and has proper child shapes $T_1,\ldots,T_s$. We will condition one step before $e$. Use $S$ as the old tree, designate one of its leaves as a marker anchor, and add a full copy of $S$ all of whose leaves split from the anchor at $e-1$. Its first path must be fresh at $e-1$, because the old tree has not yet branched there. All other target paths lie in the resulting fresh subtree. The extension coefficient on both sides of the prescribed-tree identity is the same nonzero number <a id="decor:marker-coefficient"></a>$$C(S,e)=-\delta \kappa(S).
 \label{decor:marker-coefficient}$$ The target and old copies are conditionally independent and identically distributed below $\mathscr F_{e-1}$. With $f=R_S$ and $M=\mathbb E[R_S\mid\mathscr F_{e-1}]$, their product has conditional expectation $M^2$. On the anchor-alone side, deleting the marker’s continuation below $e-1$ integrates a probability kernel to one and leaves the ordinary target law $S$, with mean $\mathbb E R_S$. The identity therefore gives <a id="decor:marker-variance-identity"></a>$$C(S,e)\bigl(\mathbb EM^2-(\mathbb E R_S)^2\bigr)
 =C(S,e)\operatorname{Var}(M)=o_N(1),
 \label{decor:marker-variance-identity}$$ and hence $$\qquad \operatorname{Var}(M)\longrightarrow0
 \quad\text{at each fixed }L,S,e.
 \label{decor:prefix-projection-concentration}$$ Division by $C(S,e)$ is made only at fixed $L$; no uniform lower bound as $L\to\infty$ is needed.

It remains to control the conditional variance below this prefix. The child covariances are evaluated at $e$, which is separated from all their branching depths; the move to $e-1$ will use martingale continuity instead. Set $$G=\mathbf1\cdot\prod_j \mu_{e,T_j},
 \qquad G_-=\mathbf1\cdot\prod_j \mu_{e-1,T_j}.$$ The latter is $\mathscr F_{e-1}$-measurable. By <a href="#decor:projection-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:projection-bound">[decor:projection-bound]</a>, $\mathbb E(R_S-G)^2\le p_N(S,e)^2$. Keeping all proper child depths fixed, the product difference inequality and <a href="#decor:martingale-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:martingale-energy">[decor:martingale-energy]</a> imply <a id="decor:adjacent-prefix-error"></a>$$\mathcal A_{L,\eta}\mathbb E(G-G_-)^2\le C_k/L.
 \label{decor:adjacent-prefix-error}$$ Here $e$ is itself a ranged coordinate, so the one-step increments are summed before division by $L$. Since conditional expectation is the optimal $L^2$ projection, $$\mathbb E(R_S-M)^2
   \le\mathbb E(R_S-G_-)^2
   \le2p_N(S,e)^2+2\mathbb E(G-G_-)^2.$$ Variance decomposition and <a href="#decor:prefix-projection-concentration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:prefix-projection-concentration">[decor:prefix-projection-concentration]</a> now show <a id="decor:variance-estimate"></a>$$\limsup_{N\to\infty}
      \mathcal A_{L,\eta}\operatorname{Var}(R_S)
 \le C_k\left(L^{-1}+V_{k-1,L}(\eta/2)^{1/2}\right)
 \quad\text{for all sufficiently large }L.
 \label{decor:variance-estimate}$$ In this estimate we used $p_N(S,e)^2\le s\sum_jv_N(T_j,e)^{1/2}$ and <a href="#decor:fiber-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:fiber-bound">[decor:fiber-bound]</a> with $\gamma=1/2$: here the full average has $D=b(S)$ coordinates because $d$ is absent, while still $D_j=b(T_j)+1$. Proposition <a href="#decor:conditional-concentration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="decor:conditional-concentration">20</a> finishes the proof. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-decor-order-of-limits"></a>
<div id="decor:order-of-limits" class="remark">

*Remark 21*. All uncancelled small denominators in this section occur only in prescribed-tree identity errors. At fixed $L$ there are finitely many relevant trees, so those errors disappear before any depth average or $L$ limit is taken. Errors that survive the $N$ limit are controlled by the uniform relative absolute-cost bound <a href="#decor:relative-absolute-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="decor:relative-absolute-cost">[decor:relative-absolute-cost]</a>, the lower-order covariance averages, and the martingale estimate $C/L$. No rate of convergence in $N$ uniform in $L$ is used.

</div>

<a id="comp:section"></a>
### Independent hierarchical messages and the cavity comparison

We turn the reservoir into a trial law in three steps: encode its full one-site branching law, use multioverlap concentration to separate the sites, and compare the resulting insertion logarithms.

<a id="comp:low-increments"></a>
Fix $\varepsilon>0$. At each depth $L\ge2$, use the sequence of sizes and deterministic parameter choices supplied by <a href="#pert:selection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pert:selection">12</a>. Write $N$ for sizes on this sequence. The choices may depend on $L$, and all statements below concern these choices. Retain $\underline F$ for the liminf over *all* system sizes of the bounded model, as before selection. In particular, $$\label{comp:low-increments}
 \Delta_N^{L,\mathbf u_{L,N}}\le\underline F+2\varepsilon.$$

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
#### Encoding a single-site tree

<a id="stmt-comp-encoding"></a>
<div id="comp:encoding" class="lemma">

**Lemma 22** (Recursive law encoding). *For each fixed $N$, $L\ge2$, and selected parameter vector, with the reservoir root disorder averaged, there is a deterministic trial law $\zeta_{L,N}\in\mathcal M_L$ whose single-label spin process has the same expected spin products on every prescribed tree as a uniformly chosen site of the reservoir. Different trial labels can be chosen independently.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-comp-encoding"><summary>Proof of stmt-comp-encoding</summary>

*Proof.* Include an independent uniform site $i\in[N]$ in the root variable, along with the reservoir root disorder. Sample the tilted reservoir auxiliary kernels along each path, and let $\mathscr G_d$ be the sigma-field generated by this enlarged root and the full auxiliary prefix through depth $d$. At depth $r=L-1$ put $$x=\mathop{\mathrm{arctanh}}\langle\sigma_i\rangle,$$ where the brackets denote the leaf Gibbs expectation. This is finite: at finite $N$ all configuration weights are strictly positive, so $-1<\langle\sigma_i\rangle<1$. Thus $x$ is determined at depth $r$, before the terminal spin transition at depth $L$. At each terminal replica leaf, draw a spin with law $q_x$. Given the auxiliary prefix, these spins are independent and have exactly the one-site law of the independent terminal Gibbs replicas.

Define the random measures backward by $$\eta_{r-1}=\operatorname{Law}(x\mid\mathscr G_{r-1}),\qquad
 \eta_d=\operatorname{Law}(\eta_{d+1}\mid\mathscr G_d)
 \quad(0\le d<r-1).$$ Then $\eta_d\in\mathcal M_{r-d}$, so $\zeta_{L,N}=\operatorname{Law}(\eta_0)\in\mathcal M_L$. All state spaces are standard Borel, so these conditional probability kernels have measurable versions. The auxiliary priors used here may be taken to be finite products of finite mark spaces at each realized root; hence the same assertion also follows directly by finite sums conditional on the root counts.

<a id="comp:single-label-moments"></a>
Here is an explicit verification for every branching pattern. Fix a descendant tree $T$ and prescribe a spin $e_a$ at each terminal leaf. At depth $r$, its conditional probability is $G_T(x)=\prod_a q_x(e_a)$. At depth $d<r$, let $T_1,\ldots,T_j$ be the subtrees rooted at its children. Conditional independence of children gives the backward recursion $$G_T(\eta_d)=\prod_{\ell=1}^j
       \int G_{T_\ell}(\nu)\,\eta_d(d\nu).$$ By induction, this probability depends on the full prefix only through $\eta_d$. Thus the nested measures preserve the joint output law of every descendant tree, including unary stretches and arbitrary numbers of children. This recursion is precisely the branching version of <a href="#model:chain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:chain">[model:chain]</a>. Taking $\zeta_{L,N}=\operatorname{Law}(\eta_0)$ preserves the root mixture as well. In particular, for every subset $S$ of the leaves, $$\label{comp:single-label-moments}
 \mathbb E\prod_{a\in S}\tau^a
 =\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbb E\prod_{a\in S}\sigma_i^a
 =\mathbb ER_S.$$ Use independent copies of the entire construction, including the reservoir root and uniform site, for different labels. The resulting messages are independent of the new physical disorder in the cavity insertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-39"></a>
#### From multioverlaps to spin patterns

<a id="stmt-comp-patterns"></a>
<div id="comp:patterns" class="lemma">

**Lemma 23** (Empirical-pattern comparison). *Fix a prescribed tree with $k$ leaves. Suppose an observable of absolute value at most one uses the replica-spin patterns at $a$ independent uniform site labels. Replacing these patterns by $a$ independent trial-label patterns from <a href="#comp:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:encoding">22</a> changes its expected value by at most <a id="comp:pattern-error"></a>$$\label{comp:pattern-error}
 a\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq[k]}\mathbb E|R_S-\mathbb ER_S|.$$ The observable may also depend on new insertion disorder, independent of the reservoir.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-comp-patterns"><summary>Proof of stmt-comp-patterns</summary>

*Proof.* Conditional on the sampled reservoir configurations, the pattern $\boldsymbol\sigma_i=(\sigma_i^1,\ldots,\sigma_i^k)$ at a uniform site has empirical distribution $$\pi(\boldsymbol e)=\frac1N\sum_i
 \prod_{b=1}^k\frac{1+e_b\sigma_i^b}{2}
 =2^{-k}\sum_{S\subseteq[k]}R_S\prod_{b\in S}e_b,
 \qquad \boldsymbol e\in\{-1,1\}^k.$$ The trial-label distribution is $\bar\pi=\mathbb E\pi$ by <a href="#comp:single-label-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:single-label-moments">[comp:single-label-moments]</a>. Independent uniform sampling with replacement gives $\pi^{\otimes a}$ conditionally in the reservoir; the trial distribution is $\bar\pi^{\otimes a}$. The elementary telescoping bound for product measures gives $$\lVert\pi^{\otimes a}-\bar\pi^{\otimes a}\rVert_{\rm TV}
 \le a\lVert\pi-\bar\pi\rVert_{\rm TV}
 \le\frac a2\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq[k]}|R_S-\mathbb ER_S|.$$ Integrating a function bounded by one costs at most twice total variation. Conditioning on the independent insertion disorder proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="comp:shape-error"></a>
<a id="comp:shape-error-bound"></a>
Let $Q_k$ be the tree law in <a href="#tree:log-expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tree:log-expansion">3</a>. We need the following consequence of the decorrelation theorem: $$\label{comp:shape-error}
 \lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty}
 \mathbb E_{Q_k}\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq[k]}
 \mathbb E|R_S-\mathbb ER_S|=0\qquad(k\text{ fixed}).$$ Here and below the inner limit is along the selected fixed-$L$ sequence. To see this, fix $0<\eta<1/2$, and let $\mathfrak S_k$ be the finite collection of labeled topologies on nonempty subsets of $[k]$. We claim the bound $$\label{comp:shape-error-bound}
 \mathbb E_{Q_k}\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq[k]}
       \mathbb E|R_S-\mathbb ER_S|
 \le C_k\left(\eta+L^{-1}
       +\sum_{U\in\mathfrak S_k}
          \bigl(\mathcal A_{L,\eta}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(R_U)\bigr)^{1/2}\right).$$ The average for a singleton has no depth coordinates. By <a href="#tree:regular-shapes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tree:regular-shapes">7</a>, nonregular trees have probability at most $C_k(\eta+L^{-1})$. On regular trees every induced subtree retains the interior and separation conditions on its branching depths. For a topology with $a$ branching vertices, each depth assignment has probability at most $C_kL^{-a}$. If an induced subtree retains $a'$ branching vertices, each assignment of its depths has at most $L^{a-a'}$ extensions to the full tree. Summing out these unused coordinates leaves exactly the normalization $L^{-a'}$ in $\mathcal A_{L,\eta}$. Cauchy–Schwarz bounds the resulting average of mean absolute deviations by $\bigl(\mathcal A_{L,\eta}\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(R_U)\bigr)^{1/2}$. Summing over the finitely many topologies and subsets, and using the bound two for each deviation on nonregular trees, proves <a href="#comp:shape-error-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:shape-error-bound">[comp:shape-error-bound]</a>. Apply <a href="#decor:overlap-concentration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="decor:overlap-concentration">17</a> first at fixed $\eta$, taking the fixed-$L$ limit in $N$ before $L\to\infty$, and then let $\eta\downarrow0$ to obtain <a href="#comp:shape-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:shape-error">[comp:shape-error]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
#### Comparison of the insertion logarithms

<a id="stmt-comp-insertion-comparison"></a>
<div id="comp:insertion-comparison" class="proposition">

<a id="comp:increment-comparison"></a>
**Proposition 24**. *For the trial laws of <a href="#comp:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:encoding">22</a>, with $\mathbf m^{(L)}=(1/L,\ldots,(L-1)/L)$, $$\label{comp:increment-comparison}
 \lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty}
 \left|\Delta_N^{L,\mathbf u_{L,N}}
 -\mathcal B_{L-1}(\zeta_{L,N},\mathbf m^{(L)})\right|=0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-comp-insertion-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-comp-insertion-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* For each insertion in <a href="#cavity:increment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cavity:increment">10</a>, replace the reservoir spins at its site labels by independent spin processes from <a href="#comp:encoding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:encoding">22</a>. Denote the resulting trial insertions by $\widetilde A_{\rm s}$ and $\widetilde A_{\rm b}$, and their recursions from <a href="#tree:Y-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:Y-recursion">[tree:Y-recursion]</a> by $\widetilde Y_{0,\rm s}$ and $\widetilde Y_{0,\rm b}$. These recursions use the trial kernels.

At the terminal spin transition, averaging $\widetilde A_{\rm s}$ over its independent trial spins gives exactly $V_{\rm s}$ in <a href="#model:vs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:vs">[model:vs]</a>; hence $\widetilde Y_{0,\rm s}=T_0V_{\rm s}$. For $\widetilde A_{\rm b}$ the same transition gives a product of $K_{\rm b}$ edge factors with disjoint message labels. Given the physical disorder and all message prefixes at a depth, these label groups remain independent. Their product therefore factors through each conditional power mean, successively from the last level to the first. Consequently, $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E\log\widetilde Y_{0,\rm b}
 &=\alpha(p-1)\mathbb E\log T_0V_{\rm e},\\
 \log2+\mathbb E\log\widetilde Y_{0,\rm s}
       -\mathbb E\log\widetilde Y_{0,\rm b}
 &=\mathcal B_{L-1}(\zeta_{L,N},\mathbf m^{(L)}).
 \end{aligned}$$ By <a href="#cavity:identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cavity:identity">[cavity:identity]</a>, it remains to compare the reservoir and trial insertion logarithms in the stated limits.

Consider either insertion and first restrict its Poisson count $K$ to $K\le M$, where $M$ is fixed. Set $C=H+BM$, $z=\cosh C$, and $c=\tanh C<1$. On this event both the reservoir insertion and its trial version can be written as $A=z(1+D)$ with $|D|\le c$, uniformly in all disorder and path variables. The deterministic term $\log z$ cancels in the comparison.

At each order $k$ of <a href="#tree:log-expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tree:log-expansion">3</a>, the product of the $k$ copies of $D$ is a bounded function of replica-spin patterns at at most $pM$ labels and of independent insertion disorder. By <a href="#comp:patterns" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:patterns">23</a>, its evaluation error on a fixed tree is bounded by $pM\sum_{S\ne\varnothing}\mathbb E|R_S-\mathbb ER_S|$. Hence <a href="#comp:shape-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:shape-error">[comp:shape-error]</a> makes its tree-averaged error tend to zero in the ordered limits. For every fixed $M$, truncating the expansion after $q$ terms leaves an error at most $\sum_{k>q}c^k/k$ on either side, uniformly in $N,L$.

Outside the count cutoff the original insertion logarithms, on both sides, are bounded by $H+BK$. Their expected contribution is therefore at most $2\mathbb E[(H+BK)\mathbf1_{\{K>M\}}]$, which tends to zero as $M\to\infty$. This proves convergence of the expected insertion logarithms: explicitly, first choose $M$ to control the Poisson tail, then $q$ to control the geometric tail, and then take the limits in $N$ and $L$ for the finitely many remaining expansion terms. Together with the trial identities above and <a href="#cavity:identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cavity:identity">[cavity:identity]</a>, this proves <a href="#comp:increment-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:increment-comparison">[comp:increment-comparison]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-comp-bounded-lower"></a>
<div id="comp:bounded-lower" class="corollary">

**Corollary 25**. *For every bounded permutation-invariant interaction law and bounded field law, with the functionals defined in <a href="#model:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:section">2</a>, $$\liminf_{N\to\infty}F_N\ge\inf_{r\ge0}\Phi_r.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-comp-bounded-lower"><summary>Proof of stmt-comp-bounded-lower</summary>

*Proof.* Write $A=\inf_{r\ge0}\Phi_r$, which is finite by <a href="#model:trial-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:trial-bounds">[model:trial-bounds]</a>. Each encoded trial value is at least $A$, so <a href="#comp:low-increments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:low-increments">[comp:low-increments]</a> gives, at every fixed $L$, $$A\le\underline F+2\varepsilon+
 \limsup_{N\to\infty}
 \left|\Delta_N^{L,\mathbf u_{L,N}}
       -\mathcal B_{L-1}(\zeta_{L,N},\mathbf m^{(L)})\right|.$$ The inner limit uses that depth’s selected sequence. Letting $L\to\infty$ in <a href="#comp:insertion-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:insertion-comparison">24</a> gives $A\le\underline F+2\varepsilon$, and then $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ proves the result. ◻

</details>

<a id="reduction:section"></a>
### Normalization, truncation, and completion of the proof

The lower bound proved in <a href="#comp:bounded-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:bounded-lower">25</a> requires bounded permutation-invariant interactions, whereas the upper bound uses the factorization and positivity hypotheses. An integrable constant shift and uniform approximation connect the two statements.

<a id="stmt-reduction-stability"></a>
<div id="reduction:stability" class="lemma">

<a id="reduction:free-stability"></a>
<a id="reduction:trial-stability"></a>
<a id="reduction:shift"></a>
**Lemma 26** (Stability). *Couple two interaction laws $\theta,\theta'$ and two field laws $h,h'$, each satisfying <a href="#model:integrability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:integrability">[model:integrability]</a> for the fixed $p$ and $\alpha$, and put $D=\mathbb E\max_s|\theta(s)-\theta'(s)|$ and $d=\mathbb E|h-h'|$. Then $$\begin{aligned}
 |F_N-F_N'|&\le\alpha D+d,\label{reduction:free-stability}\\
 |\mathcal B_r(\zeta,\mathbf m)-\mathcal B_r'(\zeta,\mathbf m)|
 &\le\alpha(2p-1)D+d\label{reduction:trial-stability}
\end{aligned}$$ uniformly in $N,r$ and all trial laws, including $r=0$. If $c$ is an integrable random constant associated with an interaction and $\widehat\theta=\theta-c$, then $$\label{reduction:shift}
 \widehat F_N=F_N-\alpha\mathbb Ec,
 \qquad\widehat{\mathcal B}_r=\mathcal B_r-\alpha\mathbb Ec.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-reduction-stability"><summary>Proof of stmt-reduction-stability</summary>

*Proof.* Use the same counts and indices in the two Hamiltonians. Their pointwise log-weight difference is bounded by the sum of the interaction sup-norm differences and field differences. The logarithm of the partition function preserves this bound, which gives <a href="#reduction:free-stability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="reduction:free-stability">[reduction:free-stability]</a>. Each cavity interaction changes by at most the corresponding sup-norm interaction difference, and so does each edge logarithm. The power means preserve these root-measurable bounds. The site term has mean $\alpha p$ interactions and the edge term has coefficient $\alpha(p-1)$, giving <a href="#reduction:trial-stability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="reduction:trial-stability">[reduction:trial-stability]</a>.

For <a href="#reduction:shift" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="reduction:shift">[reduction:shift]</a>, the physical constants factor out of the spin partition function and of every power mean. In the functional, the site term shifts by $-\alpha p\mathbb Ec$ and the edge term contributes $+\alpha(p-1)\mathbb Ec$, for a net shift $-\alpha\mathbb Ec$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-reduction-stability-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-reduction-stability</summary>

*Proof of <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>.* The multiplier $a$ may depend asymmetrically on the labeled input functions, so the original interaction law need not be permutation invariant. We first remove this multiplier by an integrable scalar shift. Set $c=\theta(1,\ldots,1)$ and $\widehat\theta=\theta-c$. The constant is integrable, since $|c|\le B(\theta)$. The factorization gives $$\widehat\theta(s)=
 \log\frac{1+b\prod_{\ell=1}^p f_\ell(s_\ell)}
 {1+b\prod_{\ell=1}^p f_\ell(1)}.$$ The multiplier $a$ has disappeared. Independence and identical distribution of the $f_\ell$, and their independence of $b$, show that the law of $\widehat\theta$ is invariant under permutations of its inputs. Moreover $\mathbb EB(\widehat\theta)\le2\mathbb EB(\theta)<\infty$.

For $K>0$, truncate each entry of $\widehat\theta$ to $[-K,K]$ and truncate $h$ to $[-K,K]$, obtaining $\widehat\theta^{(K)},h^{(K)}$. Permutation invariance is preserved. The bounded-law lower bound <a href="#comp:bounded-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:bounded-lower">25</a> applies whether or not truncation preserves the factorization. By dominated convergence, $$D_K=\mathbb E\max_s|\widehat\theta(s)-\widehat\theta^{(K)}(s)|\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad d_K=\mathbb E|h-h^{(K)}|\longrightarrow0.$$ Write $\widehat I$ for the infimum of the normalized trial functional over all depths and trial laws, and $I_K$ for the same infimum with the truncated physical laws. The uniform trial estimate gives $$|\widehat I-I_K|\le\alpha(2p-1)D_K+d_K.$$ The pressure estimate and <a href="#comp:bounded-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:bounded-lower">25</a> now imply $$\liminf_N\widehat F_N
 \ge I_K-\alpha D_K-d_K
 \ge\widehat I-2\alpha pD_K-2d_K.$$ Letting $K\to\infty$ proves $\liminf_N\widehat F_N\ge\widehat I=\inf_r\widehat\Phi_r$. Undoing the constant shift gives $\liminf_NF_N\ge\inf_r\Phi_r$. Finally <a href="#upper:bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="upper:bound">9</a> gives $\limsup_NF_N\le\inf_r\Phi_r$. Both limits agree, proving existence and <a href="#main:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:formula">[main:formula]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="models:section"></a>
### Named models and the zero-temperature limit

The standard examples of the factorization are the diluted spin models and random satisfiability models of Franz–Leone and Panchenko–Talagrand (Franz and Leone 2003; Panchenko and Talagrand 2004) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[FL03](#ref-FL03), [PT04](#ref-PT04)]. We retain the Poisson counts and sampling with replacement specified in <a href="#model:hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:hamiltonian">[model:hamiltonian]</a>.

<a id="stmt-models-spin"></a>
<div id="models:spin" class="corollary">

**Corollary 27** (Viana–Bray and diluted even-spin models). *Fix an even integer $p\ge2$, $\alpha>0$, and independent real random variables $J,H$ such that $J$ has a symmetric distribution and $\mathbb E|J|+\mathbb E|H|<\infty$. For every $\beta>0$, the model with $$\theta_\beta(s_1,\ldots,s_p)=\beta J\prod_{\ell=1}^p s_\ell,
 \qquad h_\beta=\beta H$$ satisfies the variational formula <a href="#main:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:formula">[main:formula]</a>, with the trial functional evaluated at these interaction and field laws. In particular, this holds for the Viana–Bray model (Viana and Bray 1985) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[VB85](#ref-VB85)], which is the case $p=2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-models-spin"><summary>Proof of stmt-models-spin</summary>

*Proof.* Use $$a=\cosh(\beta J),\qquad b=\tanh(\beta J),\qquad f_\ell(s)=s.$$ The functions $f_\ell$ are deterministic independent copies, and $|b|<1$ almost surely. Symmetry makes every odd moment of $b$ zero; the even moments are nonnegative. Finally, $B(\theta_\beta)=\beta|J|$ and $|h_\beta|=\beta|H|$. Thus all hypotheses of <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> hold. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-models-sat"></a>
<div id="models:sat" class="corollary">

**Corollary 28** (Soft random even-$K$ SAT). *Fix an even integer $K\ge2$ and $\alpha>0$. Let $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_K$ be independent fair signs and let $W\ge0$ be independent of the signs, with $\mathbb EW<\infty$. For every $\beta>0$, the interaction $$\theta_\beta(s_1,\ldots,s_K)
 =-\beta W\prod_{\ell=1}^K\frac{1+\varepsilon_\ell s_\ell}{2},
 \qquad h=0,$$ satisfies <a href="#main:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="main:formula">[main:formula]</a>. The choice $W=1$ is the soft random even-$K$ SAT model; $W$ gives an independent nonnegative clause weight.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-models-sat"><summary>Proof of stmt-models-sat</summary>

*Proof.* The product $I_\varepsilon(s)=\prod_\ell(1+\varepsilon_\ell s_\ell)/2$ takes values in $\{0,1\}$ and indicates a violated clause. Therefore $$e^{\theta_\beta(s)}
 =1+(e^{-\beta W}-1)I_\varepsilon(s).$$ Take $a=1$, $b=e^{-\beta W}-1$, and $f_\ell(s)=(1+\varepsilon_\ell s)/2$. The functions are independent copies and independent of $b$. Since $0\le-b<1$, the strict product condition and every positivity inequality hold. Moreover, $B(\theta_\beta)=\beta W$, so <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> applies. ◻

</details>

For the spin model, define the unscaled energy score by $$\mathcal E_N(\sigma)
 =\sum_{k=1}^{M_N}J_k\prod_{\ell=1}^p\sigma_{i_{\ell,k}}
   +\sum_{i=1}^N H_i\sigma_i.$$ For the satisfiability model, put $$\mathcal E_N(\sigma)
 =-\sum_{k=1}^{M_N}W_k
      \prod_{\ell=1}^K\frac{1+\varepsilon_{\ell,k}
       \sigma_{i_{\ell,k}}}{2}.$$ These scores and their disorder laws do not depend on $\beta$; in both cases the partition function is $Z_N(\beta)=\sum_\sigma e^{\beta\mathcal E_N(\sigma)}$. Let $\mathcal B_{r,\beta}$ denote the functional <a href="#model:functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:functional">[model:functional]</a> for the corresponding laws at inverse temperature $\beta$.

<a id="stmt-models-ground"></a>
<div id="models:ground" class="corollary">

<a id="models:ground-formula"></a>
**Corollary 29** (Zero-temperature variational limit). *For each model in [models:spin,models:sat], the limit $$g=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\mathbb E\max_\sigma\mathcal E_N(\sigma)$$ exists and satisfies $$\label{models:ground-formula}
 g=\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac1\beta
       \inf_{r\ge0}\inf_{\zeta,\mathbf m}
          \mathcal B_{r,\beta}(\zeta,\mathbf m).$$ More precisely, the expression inside the temperature limit lies in $[g,g+\log(2)/\beta]$. For unit-weight SAT, the limiting expected minimum number of violated clauses per variable is $-g$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-models-ground"><summary>Proof of stmt-models-ground</summary>

*Proof.* Write $g_N=N^{-1}\mathbb E\max_\sigma\mathcal E_N(\sigma)$ and $P_N(\beta)=N^{-1}\mathbb E\log Z_N(\beta)$. The first-moment assumptions make $g_N$ finite and uniformly bounded. Since there are $2^N$ spin configurations, $$g_N\le\frac{P_N(\beta)}\beta
       \le g_N+\frac{\log2}{\beta}.$$ For each fixed $\beta>0$, <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> gives $P_N(\beta)\to I(\beta)$, where $I(\beta)=\inf_{r,\zeta,\mathbf m}\mathcal B_{r,\beta}(\zeta,\mathbf m)$. Hence $$\limsup_N g_N\le\frac{I(\beta)}\beta
   \le\liminf_N g_N+\frac{\log2}{\beta}.$$ Letting $\beta\to\infty$ proves that $g_N$ converges to a limit $g$. Taking $N\to\infty$ in the finite-volume bounds now gives $g\le I(\beta)/\beta\le g+\log(2)/\beta$, proving <a href="#models:ground-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="models:ground-formula">[models:ground-formula]</a>. In the satisfiability model, maximizing the negative violation count is the same as minimizing that count. ◻

</details>

The infimum in <a href="#models:ground-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="models:ground-formula">[models:ground-formula]</a> is taken over all finite hierarchies separately at each positive temperature. The formula does not require attainment or a limiting hierarchy.

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-2"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 30*. The even-arity hypothesis in <a href="#models:sat" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="models:sat">28</a> excludes random 3-SAT. For that model, the companion (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 8.4) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[OpenAIThreeSAT2026](#ref-OpenAIThreeSAT2026)] gives an exact soft-pressure formula over finite rational trials for a Poisson number of clauses whose variable positions are sampled independently with replacement. Its sign-indexed message pairs need not form a probability distribution, and its trial functional differs from <a href="#model:functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:functional">[model:functional]</a>.

</div>

```json
      "caption": "One local effect of delaying a target split. The proof shifts every internal target depth and charges distinct vertices separately, even when their depths coincide. Later target colors may reuse eligible old leaves.",
```
